The paper acknowledges the connection to denoising score matching, and similar techniques have been explored in other contexts.  The novelty mainly resides in the specific application and penalization scheme within offline RL. The theoretical analysis, while helpful, could be deeper. The error bound derivation could be more rigorous, with more assumptions and better discussion.

**Significance:** PANI has the potential for practical significance due to its efficiency and ease of implementation.

*   **Strengths:** The empirical results on D4RL are compelling. PANI consistently improves performance over baselines, sometimes significantly, and in some cases, gets close to SOTA Diffusion Models. Its compatibility with existing algorithms and lower computational requirements make it attractive for real-world applications where offline RL is needed and resources are limited. The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the design choices of PANI.
*   **Weaknesses:** The reliance on D4RL benchmarks, while standard, limits the assessment of real-world applicability.  The paper would be strengthened by experiments on more diverse or challenging offline RL datasets.  The hyperparameter sensitivity of PANI is a concern, even with the proposed hybrid noise distribution. While the paper offers guidance for noise scale selection, a more automated or adaptive approach would be desirable. The comparison against diffusion-based methods could be more nuanced, accounting for factors like inference latency and memory usage in a detailed benchmark.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7**.

The paper makes a solid contribution to offline RL. The PANI method is novel enough, well-motivated, and provides a computationally efficient approach to mitigating OOD overestimation. The theoretical foundation is sound, and the empirical results demonstrate practical effectiveness on standard benchmarks.

However, the incremental nature of some of the ideas (noise injection, score matching) and the reliance on specific benchmarks prevent a higher score. The hyperparameter sensitivity, although partly addressed, also remains a concern. If the paper included more rigorous theoretical results and a demonstration on a larger diversity of datasets, a higher rating might be justified.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 0/10

### Coling-UniA at SciVQA 2025: Few-Shot Example Retrieval and Confidence-Informed Ensembling for Multimodal Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02357v1)
- **Authors**: Christian Jaumann, Annemarie Friedrich, Rainer Lienhart
- **Abstract**: This paper describes our system for the SciVQA 2025 Shared Task on Scientific Visual Question Answering. Our system employs an ensemble of two Multimodal Large Language Models and various few-shot example retrieval strategies. The model and few-shot setting are selected based on the figure and question type. We also select answers based on the models' confidence levels. On the blind test data, our system ranks third out of seven with an average F1 score of 85.12 across ROUGE-1, ROUGE-L, and BERTS. Our code is publicly available.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper describes Coling-UniA's system for the SciVQA 2025 Shared Task, which addresses Scientific Visual Question Answering. Their approach uses an ensemble of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) without fine-tuning. Key aspects include:

*   **Few-shot Example Retrieval:** Different strategies are used to retrieve relevant examples from the training data, based on question similarity or question-and-image similarity. They investigate using both filtered (same figure type) and unfiltered sets.
*   **Model Selection:** The MLLM and few-shot setting are selected based on the question and figure type of each instance. They use InternVL3-78B and Pixtral-Large-Instruct-2411.
*   **Confidence-Informed Ensembling:** Answers from a well-calibrated model/setting are selected based on a confidence threshold first. For remaining questions, another model is selected based on the identified question type.

The paper presents development results, ablation studies, and the system's rank (third) in the SciVQA 2025 challenge.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper demonstrates an ensemble method, using MLLMs in a zero/few-shot scenario without fine-tuning. They test a variety of retrieval and ensembling strategies, and their method appears fairly systematic. The novelty lies primarily in the combined application of established techniques (MLLMs, few-shot learning, ensembling) to the SciVQA task, with some focus on figure/question type specific configurations and confidence score utilization.
*   **Significance:** The work shows that MLLMs can be effectively applied to SciVQA without task-specific fine-tuning. Their focus on confidence-informed ensembling is valuable, especially for the zero/few-shot setting. The ablation studies are helpful in understanding the impact of different components (retrieval strategies, model choices, shot number, figure type filtering). The performance (3rd place) on the SciVQA shared task is solid.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Comprehensive exploration of different MLLM configurations.
    *   Well-designed ablation studies to understand the impact of individual components.
    *   Systematic approach to question/figure type ensembling.
    *   The code and data are publicly available which enhances reproducibility and encourages follow-up research.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The individual techniques are not inherently novel; the main contribution lies in the ensemble approach and configuration selection strategy for this specific task.
    *   Limited in the scope in MLLMs used.
    *   The limitation described in the paper concerning pre-exposure of MLLMs to training data on which figures in this Shared Task are based could affect the results.
    *   It's unclear the extent to which the "orcale-style setup" of providing metadata provides an advantage or whether they were able to find improvements by using the MLLMs to identify the metadata and figure type independently.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper provides a practical example of how to apply MLLMs to a specialized VQA task with limited labeled data. The focus on ensembling and confidence estimation could inspire further research in similar settings.

**Justification:**

The paper successfully applies existing techniques to a new dataset. The system works well given the limited number of examples and MLLM fine tuning, so that aspect is commendable. However, as a whole, the scientific contribution is rather incremental.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Holistic Tokenizer for Autoregressive Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02358v1)
- **Authors**: Anlin Zheng, Haochen Wang, Yucheng Zhao, Weipeng Deng, Tiancai Wang, Xiangyu Zhang, Xiaojuan Qi
- **Abstract**: The vanilla autoregressive image generation model generates visual tokens in a step-by-step fashion, which limits the ability to capture holistic relationships among token sequences. Moreover, most visual tokenizers map local image patches into latent tokens, leading to limited global information. To address this, we introduce \textit{Hita}, a novel image tokenizer for autoregressive (AR) image generation. It introduces a holistic-to-local tokenization scheme with learnable holistic queries and local patch tokens. Besides, Hita incorporates two key strategies for improved alignment with the AR generation process: 1) it arranges a sequential structure with holistic tokens at the beginning followed by patch-level tokens while using causal attention to maintain awareness of previous tokens; and 2) before feeding the de-quantized tokens into the decoder, Hita adopts a lightweight fusion module to control information flow to prioritize holistic tokens. Extensive experiments show that Hita accelerates the training speed of AR generators and outperforms those trained with vanilla tokenizers, achieving \textbf{2.59 FID} and \textbf{281.9 IS} on the ImageNet benchmark. A detailed analysis of the holistic representation highlights its ability to capture global image properties such as textures, materials, and shapes. Additionally, Hita also demonstrates effectiveness in zero-shot style transfer and image in-painting. The code is available at \href{https://github.com/CVMI-Lab/Hita}{https://github.com/CVMI-Lab/Hita}
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Holistic Tokenizer for Autoregressive Image Generation" (Hita):

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Hita, a novel image tokenizer designed to improve autoregressive (AR) image generation. The core idea is to move beyond patch-based tokenization, which the authors argue limits the ability to capture global image properties and semantic information. Hita uses a "holistic-to-local" tokenization scheme. This involves extracting global image features using learnable queries (enhanced with semantics from a pre-trained foundation model like DINOv2) and then combining them with patch-level tokens. Hita is explicitly designed to align with the sequential nature of AR generation by: (1) placing holistic tokens at the beginning of the token sequence and (2) employing a lightweight fusion module that prioritizes holistic tokens during image reconstruction. Experiments demonstrate that Hita accelerates training and improves image quality compared to tokenizers trained in the vanilla manner, achieving state-of-the-art FID and IS scores on ImageNet for AR image generation. The paper also showcases Hita's effectiveness in zero-shot style transfer and in-painting tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the holistic-to-local tokenization scheme, particularly the use of learnable queries to capture global image properties and the integration of a pre-trained foundation model to inject semantic information. While the concept of combining global and local features isn't entirely new, the specific implementation within a tokenization framework for AR image generation appears to be a significant contribution. The focus on explicitly addressing the limitations of patch-based tokenization in the context of sequential AR models is also a valuable aspect. The sequential order of the global and local tokens is also significant. The holistic tokens serve as a prompt to improve performance, and the causal architecture makes the training aligned with the AR generation framework.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance is multi-faceted:

    *   **Improved AR Image Generation:** The superior FID/IS scores demonstrate a tangible improvement in image quality compared to existing AR generation techniques, especially those based on LlamaGen.
    *   **Accelerated Training:** The faster convergence during training has practical implications, reducing the computational cost of developing and deploying AR image generation models.
    *   **Zero-Shot Capabilities:** The demonstration of zero-shot style transfer and in-painting capabilities highlights the robustness and generalizability of the learned holistic representation.
    *   **Framework Integration:** The design enables seamless integration with standard AR transformer models, increasing usability to those with existing systems.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of patch-based tokenization in AR image generation.
    *   **Well-Defined Solution:** Hita provides a coherent and well-motivated solution to address these limitations.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experiments are comprehensive, with detailed comparisons to state-of-the-art methods across multiple metrics. The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of different components.
    *   **Code Availability:** The open-source release enhances reproducibility and facilitates further research.
    *   The visualization showcases that it can capture global style and global content information for image generation.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on Foundation Model:** The reliance on a pre-trained foundation model (DINOv2) might limit the generality of the approach. The paper could benefit from a more thorough exploration of the impact of different foundation models or training without one altogether.
    *   **Limited Dataset:** Although the authors claimed they limited the dataset to achieve fair comparision with LlamaGen, they mentioned it can also be a limitation of Hita and can affect the performance.
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** While the improvements are significant, they might be viewed as incremental in the context of rapid progress in generative modeling.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   **New Tokenization Paradigms:** Hita could inspire new tokenization strategies that explicitly incorporate global image properties and semantic information.
    *   **Improved AR Architectures:** The focus on alignment with AR generation could lead to more efficient and effective AR architectures for image synthesis.
    *   **Foundation for Transfer Learning:** The holistic representation learned by Hita could serve as a foundation for transfer learning to other image generation tasks or domains.

*   **Overall Assessment:**

    The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to image tokenization for AR generation. The results are compelling, and the detailed analysis provides valuable insights. While there are some limitations, the paper represents a significant contribution to the field and has the potential to influence future research in image generation.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

A score of 8 reflects the paper's significant contributions, clear presentation, comprehensive experimentation, and potential impact on the field. It is not scored higher due to the dependence on a pre-trained foundation model, the limitation in the dataset used, and the incremental nature of the improvement in the context of rapid progress in the field. The paper is well-executed and provides a solid foundation for future research, but it is not a paradigm shift that would warrant a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### QFFN-BERT: An Empirical Study of Depth, Performance, and Data Efficiency in Hybrid Quantum-Classical Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02364v1)
- **Authors**: Pilsung Kang
- **Abstract**: Parameterized quantum circuits (PQCs) have recently emerged as promising components for enhancing the expressibility of neural architectures. In this work, we introduce QFFN-BERT, a hybrid quantum-classical transformer where the feedforward network (FFN) modules of a compact BERT variant are replaced by PQC-based layers. This design is motivated by the dominant parameter contribution of FFNs, which account for approximately two-thirds of the parameters within standard Transformer encoder blocks. While prior studies have primarily integrated PQCs into self-attention modules, our work focuses on the FFN and systematically investigates the trade-offs between PQC depth, expressibility, and trainability. Our final PQC architecture incorporates a residual connection, both $R_Y$ and $R_Z$ rotations, and an alternating entanglement strategy to ensure stable training and high expressibility. Our experiments, conducted on a classical simulator, on the SST-2 and DBpedia benchmarks demonstrate two key findings. First, a carefully configured QFFN-BERT achieves up to 102.0% of the baseline accuracy, surpassing its classical counterpart in a full-data setting while reducing FFN-specific parameters by over 99%. Second, our model exhibits a consistent and competitive edge in few-shot learning scenarios, confirming its potential for superior data efficiency. These results, supported by an ablation study on a non-optimized PQC that failed to learn, confirm that PQCs can serve as powerful and parameter-efficient alternatives to classical FFNs when co-designed with foundational deep learning principles.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will summarize and critically evaluate the paper.

**Summary:**

The paper "QFFN-BERT: An Empirical Study of Depth, Performance, and Data Efficiency in Hybrid Quantum-Classical Transformers" explores the integration of Parameterized Quantum Circuits (PQCs) into the Feedforward Network (FFN) modules of a compact BERT model. The authors propose a hybrid quantum-classical architecture, QFFN-BERT, where the FFNs are replaced by PQC-based layers, aiming to improve parameter efficiency and generalization. They systematically vary the PQC depth to investigate the trade-offs between expressibility, trainability, and generalization. Experiments on SST-2 and DBpedia datasets demonstrate that a carefully configured QFFN-BERT can surpass the classical baseline in full-data settings and exhibit a competitive edge in few-shot learning, while significantly reducing FFN-specific parameters. The work also includes an ablation study highlighting the importance of co-design principles, such as residual connections and sophisticated PQC designs, for successful integration of quantum circuits in deep learning models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of integrating PQCs into neural networks, particularly transformers, is not entirely new. However, this paper's novelty lies in its **specific focus on the FFN module, a comparatively less explored area compared to attention mechanisms**, and in its systematic empirical investigation of the depth-expressibility-trainability trade-offs in this context. The detailed ablation study and the concrete implementation of QFFN-BERT also contribute to the novelty. While the individual components (PQCs, transformers) are well-established, the specific combination and the empirical analysis offer a fresh perspective.

*   **Significance:** The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of quantum machine learning, especially in the context of natural language processing. By demonstrating that PQCs can effectively replace classical FFNs in transformers, the authors provide evidence for the potential of quantum-enhanced models to achieve competitive performance with significantly reduced parameter counts. The empirical analysis of the depth trade-offs and the importance of co-design principles offers practical guidance for future research in this area. The results are especially significant in the context of resource-constrained environments or scenarios where data efficiency is crucial. While full replacement of FFN structures with quantum circuits is not currently feasible under the limitations of NISQ-era hardware, the insights into expressivity and data efficiency could remain feasible within simulated or limited-qubit environments.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Systematic Evaluation:** The paper presents a well-designed and systematic experimental evaluation, varying PQC depth and analyzing performance across different datasets and training scenarios (full-data, few-shot).
    *   **Ablation Study:** The ablation study provides strong evidence for the importance of specific architectural choices, such as residual connections, highlighting the need for careful co-design.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly presents the methodology, results, and analysis.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The authors describe the experimental setup in sufficient detail, promoting reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Cost:** The CPU-based simulation limits the scale of the experiments. Running on a simulator is an inherent limitation, not something the authors did, however, the use of small models and the fact that results are still in simulation bring the overall score down. The paper mentions the computational burden of the PQC module. This limits its practical applicability in the near term.
    *   **Limited Scope:** The study focuses on relatively small models (bert-tiny) and a limited set of datasets. The generalizability of the results to larger models and more diverse tasks remains an open question.
    *   **NISQ Feasibility:** While the paper acknowledges the limitations of NISQ-era hardware, it would be beneficial to discuss more explicitly the challenges of deploying the proposed architecture on real quantum devices.  What kind of error mitigation strategies might be necessary?  How would qubit connectivity constraints affect the design?

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could stimulate further research into hybrid quantum-classical architectures for NLP, particularly focusing on the optimization of PQC designs and the development of co-design principles. The findings could also inform the development of more data-efficient and resource-efficient NLP models. The strong, deliberate design choices in their approach serve as a guide to other researchers developing such systems.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper makes a novel and useful contribution to the field, but with limitations. Therefore, a score that reflects some degree of influence is appropriate.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### UVLM: Benchmarking Video Language Model for Underwater World Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02373v1)
- **Authors**: Xizhe Xue, Yang Zhou, Dawei Yan, Ying Li, Haokui Zhang, Rong Xiao
- **Abstract**: Recently, the remarkable success of large language models (LLMs) has achieved a profound impact on the field of artificial intelligence. Numerous advanced works based on LLMs have been proposed and applied in various scenarios. Among them, video language models (VidLMs) are particularly widely used. However, existing works primarily focus on terrestrial scenarios, overlooking the highly demanding application needs of underwater observation. To overcome this gap, we introduce UVLM, an under water observation benchmark which is build through a collaborative approach combining human expertise and AI models. To ensure data quality, we have conducted in-depth considerations from multiple perspectives. First, to address the unique challenges of underwater environments, we selected videos that represent typical underwater challenges including light variations, water turbidity, and diverse viewing angles to construct the dataset. Second, to ensure data diversity, the dataset covers a wide range of frame rates, resolutions, 419 classes of marine animals, and various static plants and terrains. Next, for task diversity, we adopted a structured design where observation targets are categorized into two major classes: biological and environmental. Each category includes content observation and change/action observation, totaling 20 distinct task types. Finally, we designed several challenging evaluation metrics to enable quantitative comparison and analysis of different methods. Experiments on two representative VidLMs demonstrate that fine-tuning VidLMs on UVLM significantly improves underwater world understanding while also showing potential for slight improvements on existing in-air VidLM benchmarks, such as VideoMME and Perception text. The dataset and prompt engineering will be released publicly.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the UVLM paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces UVLM, a new benchmark dataset specifically designed for video language models (VidLMs) to understand underwater environments. The authors argue that existing VidLMs are primarily focused on terrestrial scenarios and lack the ability to handle the unique challenges presented by underwater video, such as poor visibility, light attenuation, and lack of domain-specific knowledge. UVLM aims to address this gap by providing a comprehensive dataset of underwater videos with detailed annotations covering both biological and environmental aspects. The dataset includes a diverse range of marine life, environmental conditions, and task types, as well as specialized evaluation metrics. The paper presents experiments that demonstrate the effectiveness of fine-tuning VidLMs on UVLM for underwater understanding.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's main novelty lies in the creation of a dedicated benchmark dataset for underwater video understanding. While datasets exist for underwater image enhancement, object detection, and other related tasks, UVLM appears to be the first large-scale video-language dataset specifically focused on this domain. This addresses a real gap in the field, as highlighted by the authors. The structured approach to data collection, annotation and the use of human-AI collaboration to ensure data quality also represents a positive contribution.

*   **Significance:** The significance of UVLM lies in its potential to advance the field of underwater video understanding. By providing a standardized benchmark, it enables researchers to develop and evaluate VidLMs specifically designed for this challenging environment. This is particularly important given the increasing use of underwater robotics, environmental monitoring, and other applications that rely on accurate analysis of underwater imagery. The improvements demonstrated by finetuning existing models, and potential for better performance with models specifically designed for this task also underscore the paper's significance.
    *   The datasets will also assist the development of underwater autonomy by providing additional high-level scene understanding which can complement existing low-level autonomy tasks (tracking, scene reconstruction, etc.)

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined problem:** The paper clearly identifies the challenges of applying existing VidLMs to underwater scenarios.
    *   **Comprehensive dataset:** UVLM appears to be a well-curated and comprehensive dataset with diverse content, annotations, and task types.
    *   **Structured approach:** The authors adopted a structured methodology for data collection, annotation, and evaluation, which enhances the reproducibility and reliability of the benchmark.
    *   **Demonstrated improvements:** The experimental results show that fine-tuning VidLMs on UVLM significantly improves their performance on underwater understanding tasks.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Baseline comparison:** The paper experiments with two selected VidLMs, and it would have been more impactful to experiment with a larger number of different models, particularly those specifically designed for underwater tasks.
    *   **Scalability concerns:** The data collection relies in large part on manual annotation, which could limit its scalability to larger datasets.
    *   **Potential bias:** Although the authors discuss data diversity, there is still the potential for bias in the dataset due to factors such as the geographical location of the videos or the types of marine life represented. The authors should elaborate how any domain experts verified for this and how to mitigate it.

*   **Impact:** UVLM has the potential to have a significant impact on the field of underwater video understanding. It provides a valuable resource for researchers and engineers working on this challenging problem, and the benchmark could help to drive innovation and accelerate progress. There may be a spillover benefit to other low-quality imaging tasks.

*   **Justification of Score:**

    While the paper addresses a clear need and presents a well-constructed dataset, the limited experimental validation and potential scalability concerns prevent it from achieving a higher score. The paper's impact will depend on its adoption by the research community and the extent to which it facilitates the development of more capable underwater VidLMs. The benchmark contributes a timely and necessary tool in a growing field, and the fine-grained annotation scheme allows for significant progress in automated underwater scene understanding.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Efficient Code LLM Training via Distribution-Consistent and Diversity-Aware Data Selection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02378v1)
- **Authors**: Weijie Lyu, Sheng-Jun Huang, Xuan Xia
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in large language models (LLMs) have significantly improved code generation and program comprehension, accelerating the evolution of software engineering. Current methods primarily enhance model performance by leveraging vast amounts of data, focusing on data quantity while often overlooking data quality, thereby reducing training efficiency. To address this, we introduce an approach that utilizes a parametric model for code data selection, aimed at improving both training efficiency and model performance. Our method optimizes the parametric model to ensure distribution consistency and diversity within the selected subset, guaranteeing high-quality data. Experimental results demonstrate that using only 10K samples, our method achieves gains of 2.4% (HumanEval) and 2.3% (MBPP) over 92K full-sampled baseline, outperforming other sampling approaches in both performance and efficiency. This underscores that our method effectively boosts model performance while significantly reducing computational costs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel data selection approach for training code Large Language Models (LLMs).  It uses a parametric model to select a subset of training data that maintains distribution consistency with the original dataset while maximizing diversity within the selected subset.  The core idea is to map data samples into a feature space, optimize a parametric model using a loss function that balances distribution alignment and diversity, and then select samples closest to the optimized parameters.  Experiments demonstrate that training on a small, carefully selected subset (10K samples) can outperform training on the full dataset (92K samples) and other sampling methods, both in terms of model performance (HumanEval and MBPP benchmarks) and training efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in introducing a parametric model for data selection in the *code* LLM training domain.  While the concept of using parametric models for active learning or data selection is not entirely new (as acknowledged through the reference to ActiveFT), its application to code-specific data selection, combined with the specific loss function balancing distribution consistency and diversity, is a valuable contribution. Prior works on data selection for LLMs primarily focused on general tasks or used external oracles (e.g., ChatGPT) which can be economically prohibitive. The idea of optimizing for both distribution alignment *and* diversity simultaneously within the feature space is a strength.

* **Significance:** The results are significant in demonstrating that data quality can outweigh quantity in code LLM training. The reported gains of 2.4% on HumanEval and 2.3% on MBPP using only 10K selected samples compared to 92K samples represents a substantial improvement in training efficiency and model performance. This has practical implications for researchers and practitioners who have limited computational resources.  The improved sampling efficiency compared to other methods (e.g., DQ, DEITA) is also a valuable result, indicating the scalability of the approach. However, it is important to note that all sampling strategies outperform a naive approach (training with the same number of samples) in the original distribution which makes the comparison to the full dataset even more meaningful.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition and Solution:** The paper clearly defines the problem of inefficient code LLM training and proposes a well-motivated solution.
    * **Strong Experimental Results:** The experimental results are convincing, showing consistent improvements over baselines in both performance and efficiency. The ablation study varying the sampling quantity provides additional insights.
    * **Well-Written and Organized:** The paper is well-written, making it easy to understand the methodology and results.
    * **Efficient Sampling Time:** The low sampling time required by their approach is a strong advantage.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Scope:** The experimental validation is primarily limited to Python-specific benchmarks and the DeepSeekCoder-Base-6.7B model.  Generalizability to other programming languages and larger models needs further investigation.
    * **Functional Correctness:** The method does not directly validate the functional correctness of the selected code, which could potentially introduce flawed samples into the training set. This is acknowledged by the authors as a limitation.
    * **Reliance on a Pre-trained Model:** The method relies on a pre-trained sentence transformer (all-mpnet-base-v2) for feature encoding. While this is a reasonable choice, the performance of the method might be sensitive to the choice of this encoder.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by:
    * Encouraging researchers to focus on data quality and efficient data selection strategies for code LLM training.
    * Providing a practical and scalable approach for improving code LLM performance with limited computational resources.
    * Inspiring further research on integrating parametric models and feature-based approaches into data selection for other LLM domains.

**Score:** 7.5

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and practical approach for data selection in code LLM training, with strong experimental results demonstrating significant improvements in both performance and efficiency. While the scope of the evaluation is limited, the potential impact of the work on the field is substantial. The use of parametric models for code data selection, coupled with the diversity constraint is a significant step in the right direction. Future work should address the limitations related to functional correctness and generalizability to other programming languages and larger models. However, this paper provides a tangible contribution to the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### JoyTTS: LLM-based Spoken Chatbot With Voice Cloning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02380v1)
- **Authors**: Fangru Zhou, Jun Zhao, Guoxin Wang
- **Abstract**: JoyTTS is an end-to-end spoken chatbot that combines large language models (LLM) with text-to-speech (TTS) technology, featuring voice cloning capabilities. This project is built upon the open-source MiniCPM-o and CosyVoice2 models and trained on 2000 hours of conversational data. We have also provided the complete training code to facilitate further development and optimization by the community. On the testing machine seed-tts-zh, it achieves a SS (speaker similarity) score of 0.73 and a WER (Word Error Rate) of 5.09. The code and models, along with training and inference scripts, are available at https://github.com/jdh-algo/JoyTTS.git.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces JoyTTS, an end-to-end spoken chatbot that integrates a large language model (LLM) with text-to-speech (TTS) technology and voice cloning capabilities. Built upon the open-source MiniCPM-o and CosyVoice2 models, JoyTTS is trained on a 2000-hour conversational dataset. The system incorporates a tokenizer module, an LLM-Chat module using the Qwen-7B model, an LLM-TTS module, and a generator module.  The authors describe the training process, including data augmentation techniques and a two-stage training approach. They report performance metrics (speaker similarity and word error rate) on the seed-tts-zh dataset and discuss the computational efficiency of the system. The authors make their code and models publicly available. They claim JoyTTS offers improvements in voice cloning naturalness and accuracy compared to the original TTS module within MiniCPM-o. They suggest future work involves adding emotion control inputs to the LLM.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty is limited. End-to-end spoken chatbots, especially those incorporating LLMs and TTS with voice cloning, are not entirely new. The specific contribution lies in combining existing open-source components (MiniCPM-o and CosyVoice2) and training them on a custom dataset.  The technique of using hidden states from the LLM to inform the TTS module is a reasonable approach, but it's not fundamentally groundbreaking. The improvements demonstrated over the original MiniCPM-o's TTS are expected given the swap to CosyVoice2, which is itself a more advanced TTS model. The key question is how the new system compares to *other* state-of-the-art LLM-TTS systems, and this is not sufficiently addressed. The paper misses the opportunity to provide a compelling case for its novelty.

**Significance:** The significance is moderate. The open-sourcing of the code, models, and training scripts is a positive contribution to the community. The reported performance metrics provide a baseline for future research. However, the lack of rigorous comparison against other existing systems limits the perceived impact. Without a strong comparative analysis, it's difficult to assess whether JoyTTS represents a substantial advancement over existing solutions or merely a practical implementation of known techniques. The performance results are not very competitive.

**Strengths:**

*   **Open Source:** The availability of code and models is a significant strength, facilitating reproducibility and further research.
*   **Clear Architecture:** The paper provides a reasonably clear description of the system architecture.
*   **Dataset Description:** The paper details the dataset construction and data augmentation techniques.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Novelty:** The overall approach lacks substantial novelty; the system is essentially a combination of existing open-source components.
*   **Insufficient Benchmarking:** The paper fails to provide a thorough comparison with other state-of-the-art LLM-TTS systems. The baseline comparisons focus too much on improvement versus an initial implementation and insufficiently on a comparison to *alternatives*.
*   **Modest Performance:** The reported performance is weak in certain respects. The reported word error rate (WER) result is somewhat mediocre.
*   **Limited Evaluation:** The evaluation is limited to a single dataset (seed-tts-zh).
*   **Claims of improvement could be more supported by experiments:** While the paper claims the system improves voice cloning performance, this is mostly just due to swapping components.

**Justification for the score:**

The paper presents a practical implementation of an LLM-based spoken chatbot with voice cloning. However, the combination of existing components and the absence of convincing benchmarking against other state-of-the-art systems significantly limit its novelty and significance. While the open-sourcing of the code is commendable, the lack of a strong performance case makes it less likely to have a major impact on the field. Thus, JoyTTS serves as a valuable contribution, and there are some positives to it (architecture, code, etc.), but these are relatively incremental. A score of 5 reflects that it's a reasonable contribution, but is not transformative.

**Score: 5**

- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 5/10

### Evaluating Language Models For Threat Detection in IoT Security Logs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02390v1)
- **Authors**: Jorge J. Tejero-Fernández, Alfonso Sánchez-Macián
- **Abstract**: Log analysis is a relevant research field in cybersecurity as they can provide a source of information for the detection of threats to networks and systems. This paper presents a pipeline to use fine-tuned Large Language Models (LLMs) for anomaly detection and mitigation recommendation using IoT security logs. Utilizing classical machine learning classifiers as a baseline, three open-source LLMs are compared for binary and multiclass anomaly detection, with three strategies: zero-shot, few-shot prompting and fine-tuning using an IoT dataset. LLMs give better results on multi-class attack classification than the corresponding baseline models. By mapping detected threats to MITRE CAPEC, defining a set of IoT-specific mitigation actions, and fine-tuning the models with those actions, the models are able to provide a combined detection and recommendation guidance.
- **Summary**: Okay, I have analyzed the provided document. Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the use of fine-tuned Large Language Models (LLMs) for threat detection and mitigation recommendation in IoT security logs. It compares the performance of traditional machine learning models (Random Forest, XGBoost, MLP, LightGBM) with three open-source LLMs (DeepSeek, LLaMA, Qwen) under three strategies: zero-shot, few-shot prompting, and fine-tuning. The study utilizes the Edge-IIoTset dataset and proposes a method to incorporate mitigation recommendations from the MITRE CAPEC framework into the LLM responses through additional fine-tuning. The core idea is to have LLMs not only detect the attack but also suggest specific mitigation strategies tailored to the IoT environment. Performance is evaluated using standard classification metrics, cosine similarity and ROUGE-L for mitigation quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** While the application of LLMs to cybersecurity tasks is not entirely new, this paper contributes by:

    *   Specifically focusing on IoT security logs, a domain characterized by variability, high dimensionality, and log instability, posing unique challenges.
    *   Proposing a pipeline that integrates threat detection with mitigation recommendation, which is a step beyond simple classification. This integration is vital for SOC operations.
    *   Comparing different LLM strategies (zero-shot, few-shot, fine-tuning) systematically. The fine-tuning with specific IoT-related actions for mitigation is a definite novel contribution.
    *   Using a labeled IoT dataset, the edge-IIoTset, which addresses the limitations in available resources suitable to the specific focus of their analysis.
*   **Significance:** The paper's findings have the potential to significantly impact the field of IoT security by:

    *   Demonstrating that fine-tuned LLMs can outperform traditional ML models in multi-class attack classification, a crucial task for effective security monitoring.
    *   Highlighting the importance of domain-specific fine-tuning to achieve high performance in log analysis, particularly in the context of IoT where log formats and semantics can change frequently.
    *   Providing a practical approach for automating threat mitigation, which can reduce the manual workload of security analysts and improve incident response times.
    *   Opening possibilities for integrating LLMs in Security Operations Centers (SOCs), reducing manual workload by prioritizing alerts, detecting threats, and generating recommendations.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   The methodology is well-defined and includes a comparative evaluation against traditional ML models.
    *   The inclusion of mitigation recommendation as part of the LLM response is a significant contribution.
    *   The use of the MITRE CAPEC framework provides a structured approach to mitigation generation.
    *   The exploration of different LLM sizes and tuning strategies provides useful insights for practical deployment.
    *   The data processing steps and clear metrics make the results reproducible and reliable.
    *   The focus on a specific dataset for IoT makes the findings applicable to this domain.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The dataset, while suitable, suffers from class imbalance, which could skew the results and potentially favour the detection of majority classes at the cost of those with smaller representation. The mitigation generation has been performed using only the DeepSeek model. Other models should be evaluated to see if those are actually general results.
    *   Limited exploration of prompt engineering techniques for zero-shot and few-shot scenarios, as more sophisticated prompts could yield better performance.
    *   Computational expense and deployment constraints of LLMs may hinder real-time deployment in resource-constrained IoT environments, the solution is not optimized for CPU only processing. The computational cost is also not clearly detailed in the experiment.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's findings could encourage further research into:

    *   Developing more robust LLM-based threat detection and mitigation systems specifically tailored for IoT environments.
    *   Exploring techniques for mitigating the impact of log instability on LLM performance.
    *   Investigating the use of LLMs for other cybersecurity tasks, such as vulnerability assessment and threat intelligence.
    *   Creation of more appropriate training data, as highlighted by class imbalance.

    Overall, the paper makes a valuable contribution to the field, particularly by focusing on the integration of threat detection and mitigation in IoT security logs using LLMs.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** While applying LLMs to cybersecurity is not groundbreaking, the focus on IoT logs and the integration of mitigation recommendations elevates the paper's novelty and potential impact. The clear methodology and comparison against traditional ML models further strengthen the paper. However, the limitations related to dataset imbalance and the use of a single language model for mitigation generation, as well as deployment costs, hold it back from a higher score. A more comprehensive evaluation of the mitigation generation across multiple models and strategies would have significantly improved the impact of the results. The novelty is mostly around integration of detection and mitigation in this environment, which improves over existing literature but is not radical.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Posterior Transition Modeling for Unsupervised Diffusion-Based Speech Enhancement
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02391v1)
- **Authors**: Mostafa Sadeghi, Jean-Eudes Ayilo, Romain Serizel, Xavier Alameda-Pineda
- **Abstract**: We explore unsupervised speech enhancement using diffusion models as expressive generative priors for clean speech. Existing approaches guide the reverse diffusion process using noisy speech through an approximate, noise-perturbed likelihood score, combined with the unconditional score via a trade-off hyperparameter. In this work, we propose two alternative algorithms that directly model the conditional reverse transition distribution of diffusion states. The first method integrates the diffusion prior with the observation model in a principled way, removing the need for hyperparameter tuning. The second defines a diffusion process over the noisy speech itself, yielding a fully tractable and exact likelihood score. Experiments on the WSJ0-QUT and VoiceBank-DEMAND datasets demonstrate improved enhancement metrics and greater robustness to domain shifts compared to both supervised and unsupervised baselines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces two novel unsupervised speech enhancement algorithms, DEPSE-IL and DEPSE-TL, that leverage diffusion models. Unlike existing methods that approximate the posterior score using noisy speech through a hyperparameter-weighted combination of the prior score and a noisy speech likelihood gradient, these algorithms directly model the conditional (reverse) transition distribution of diffusion states. DEPSE-IL integrates the diffusion prior and observation model, removing the need for hyperparameter tuning. DEPSE-TL introduces a diffusion process over the noisy speech itself, enabling exact computation of the time-dependent likelihood. Experimental results on the WSJ0-QUT and VoiceBank-DEMAND datasets show that these methods, particularly DEPSE-TL, achieve improved enhancement metrics and greater robustness to domain shifts compared to existing unsupervised baselines, even matching or exceeding the performance of supervised methods in mismatched conditions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**  The core novelty lies in explicitly modeling the conditional reverse transition distribution within the diffusion framework for unsupervised speech enhancement. This is a departure from traditional score-based guidance approaches that rely on approximations of the likelihood term and a hyperparameter for balancing the prior and likelihood. The introduction of a diffusion process over the noisy speech itself (DEPSE-TL) to obtain a tractable likelihood represents a significant contribution. This addresses a key limitation of previous unsupervised diffusion-based SE methods.

**Significance:** The significance of this work is multifold:

*   **Improved Performance:** The empirical results demonstrate clear improvements in speech quality, intelligibility, and robustness to domain shifts compared to existing unsupervised methods.
*   **Reduced Reliance on Hyperparameter Tuning:**  DEPSE-IL's elimination of the balancing hyperparameter, a common pain point in diffusion-based methods, simplifies the training and deployment process.
*   **Exact Likelihood Calculation:**  DEPSE-TL's tractable likelihood opens up new avenues for exploring and optimizing diffusion models in speech enhancement and potentially other audio processing tasks.
*   **Robustness to Domain Mismatch:** The experiments revealing superior robustness to domain mismatch versus supervised methods are extremely compelling and directly address a current weakness of supervised models.

**Strengths:**

*   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed algorithms and their theoretical motivations.
*   The experimental setup is comprehensive, covering a range of datasets and evaluation metrics.
*   The results demonstrate consistent and significant improvements over existing unsupervised baselines.
*   The robustness to domain shifts is a particularly valuable finding.
* The algorithm itself is well motivated theoretically and makes practical advancements to previous diffusion-based approaches.

**Weaknesses:**

*   While the paper presents comparisons with supervised methods, it admits that the baselines are not state-of-the-art. Stronger SOTA supervised comparisons would provide a more complete picture of the relative performance. This point is also fair since the focus is domain robustness and not necessarily beating SOTA.
*   The computational complexity of the proposed algorithms is not explicitly discussed. Diffusion models are generally computationally intensive, and it would be valuable to understand the trade-offs between performance and computational cost.
*   The paper focuses on single-channel speech denoising. It would be interesting to explore the applicability of these algorithms to more complex scenarios, such as multi-channel speech enhancement or speech separation.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper represents a significant advancement in unsupervised speech enhancement. The explicit modeling of the conditional transition distribution, particularly the introduction of a diffusion process over the noisy speech (DEPSE-TL), is a novel and impactful idea. The experimental results demonstrate clear improvements in performance and robustness compared to existing methods. The weaknesses mentioned (lack of SOTA comparisons and computational complexity analysis) are not fatal flaws, given the paper's primary focus on improving unsupervised methods and robustness under domain mismatch. The strengths substantially outweigh the weaknesses.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 8/10

### PosDiffAE: Position-aware Diffusion Auto-encoder For High-Resolution Brain Tissue Classification Incorporating Artifact Restoration
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02405v1)
- **Authors**: Ayantika Das, Moitreya Chaudhuri, Koushik Bhat, Keerthi Ram, Mihail Bota, Mohanasankar Sivaprakasam
- **Abstract**: Denoising diffusion models produce high-fidelity image samples by capturing the image distribution in a progressive manner while initializing with a simple distribution and compounding the distribution complexity. Although these models have unlocked new applicabilities, the sampling mechanism of diffusion does not offer means to extract image-specific semantic representation, which is inherently provided by auto-encoders. The encoding component of auto-encoders enables mapping between a specific image and its latent space, thereby offering explicit means of enforcing structures in the latent space. By integrating an encoder with the diffusion model, we establish an auto-encoding formulation, which learns image-specific representations and offers means to organize the latent space. In this work, First, we devise a mechanism to structure the latent space of a diffusion auto-encoding model, towards recognizing region-specific cellular patterns in brain images. We enforce the representations to regress positional information of the patches from high-resolution images. This creates a conducive latent space for differentiating tissue types of the brain. Second, we devise an unsupervised tear artifact restoration technique based on neighborhood awareness, utilizing latent representations and the constrained generation capability of diffusion models during inference. Third, through representational guidance and leveraging the inference time steerable noising and denoising capability of diffusion, we devise an unsupervised JPEG artifact restoration technique.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PosDiffAE, a novel position-aware denoising diffusion auto-encoder designed for high-resolution brain tissue classification and artifact restoration in Nissl-stained images. The core idea is to structure the latent space of a diffusion auto-encoding model by enforcing representations to regress positional information of patches, facilitating the differentiation of brain tissue types. The paper also presents unsupervised methods for tear artifact and JPEG artifact restoration, leveraging neighborhood awareness and inference-time steerable noising/denoising capabilities. The model's effectiveness is validated through experiments demonstrating multi-tasking capabilities (region classification, position regression, artifact restoration), robustness to artifacts, and generalization across fetal and adult human brains.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The integration of positional information into the latent space of a diffusion autoencoder for brain tissue classification is a key novel aspect. While diffusion autoencoders and latent space manipulations are not entirely new concepts, the specific application to organizing latent space for region-specific cellular patterns, coupled with unsupervised artifact restoration techniques, demonstrates a unique contribution. The idea of regressing positional information from latent representations to guide tissue type differentiation is creative and not commonly explored. The use of contextual information from neighboring patches for unsupervised tear artifact restoration adds another layer of novelty, aiming for consistent results across high-resolution images. While DDRM does JPEG artifact restoration, The PosDiffAE integrates contextual guidance which is essential in HR Histological imaging, this contextualization differentiates it from other approaches.

* **Significance:**  The paper addresses a significant challenge in digital pathology: automating the analysis of high-resolution brain tissue images, a crucial step in understanding brain development and disease. The ability to accurately classify brain regions and restore image artifacts in an unsupervised manner has the potential to reduce the need for manual annotation, enabling large-scale studies.  The demonstration of generalization across fetal and adult brains, as well as across different data acquisition sites, is particularly important for practical applications. The model potentially aids in clinical diagnosis, interpretation and staging of developmental neurological conditions.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Comprehensive Approach:** The paper covers multiple aspects of brain tissue image analysis, including region classification, position regression, and artifact restoration.
    * **Unsupervised Learning:** The unsupervised nature of the artifact restoration techniques is a significant advantage, reducing the need for labeled training data.
    * **Generalization Ability:** The experiments show that the model generalizes well across different age groups (fetal and adult) and datasets.
    * **Thorough Evaluation:**  The paper includes a wide range of evaluation metrics (Precision, Recall, Accuracy, MSE, FCD, PSNR, SSIM) and comparisons to several baselines. The statistical analysis adds rigor to the evaluation.
    * **Clear Structure and Presentation:** The paper is well-written, with clear explanations of the methods and experiments.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Computational Cost:** The paper mentions the inference time required by all DDM-based methodologies, which can be high and requires specialized hardware, limiting practical applications.
    * **Limited Artifact Types:** The paper focuses on tear and JPEG compression artifacts. Other artifacts common in histological images (e.g., staining variations, folding, debris) are not addressed.
    * **Qualitative Examples:** While quantitative metrics are thoroughly addressed, more compelling visual examples showcasing the improved artifact restoration compared to other methods in the HR setting would further strengthen the paper.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by:
    * Encouraging further research on latent space organization in diffusion models for medical image analysis.
    * Providing a foundation for developing fully automated brain tissue analysis pipelines.
    * Inspiring the development of more robust and generalizable artifact restoration techniques.

* **Score Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to a relevant problem in digital pathology.  It demonstrates clear improvements over existing methods and provides a solid foundation for future work.  However, the limited scope of artifact types addressed and the potential computational challenges prevent it from being a truly groundbreaking contribution. I therefore score it a strong 7, indicating a valuable and significant contribution to the field.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Improving Consistency in Vehicle Trajectory Prediction Through Preference Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02406v1)
- **Authors**: Caio Azevedo, Lina Achaji, Stefano Sabatini, Nicola Poerio, Grzegorz Bartyzel, Sascha Hornauer, Fabien Moutarde
- **Abstract**: Trajectory prediction is an essential step in the pipeline of an autonomous vehicle. Inaccurate or inconsistent predictions regarding the movement of agents in its surroundings lead to poorly planned maneuvers and potentially dangerous situations for the end-user. Current state-of-the-art deep-learning-based trajectory prediction models can achieve excellent accuracy on public datasets. However, when used in more complex, interactive scenarios, they often fail to capture important interdependencies between agents, leading to inconsistent predictions among agents in the traffic scene. Inspired by the efficacy of incorporating human preference into large language models, this work fine-tunes trajectory prediction models in multi-agent settings using preference optimization. By taking as input automatically calculated preference rankings among predicted futures in the fine-tuning process, our experiments--using state-of-the-art models on three separate datasets--show that we are able to significantly improve scene consistency while minimally sacrificing trajectory prediction accuracy and without adding any excess computational requirements at inference time.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Improving Consistency in Vehicle Trajectory Prediction Through Preference Optimization" addresses the problem of inconsistent predictions in multi-agent trajectory forecasting for autonomous vehicles. It leverages the concept of preference optimization, inspired by its successful application in large language models (LLMs), to fine-tune trajectory prediction models. The core idea is to automatically rank predicted future scenarios based on a defined metric (collision rates and deviation from ground truth) and then use this ranking to refine the model using a modified SimPO (Simple Preference Optimization) loss.  The experiments show that this approach reduces collision rates and improves scene consistency without significantly sacrificing trajectory prediction accuracy or adding excessive computational overhead at inference time. The method is demonstrated on several datasets using both marginal and joint trajectory prediction models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in adapting preference optimization, a technique well-established in NLP (specifically for LLMs), to the domain of trajectory prediction. While individual components like trajectory prediction models and collision detection metrics are not new, their combination within a preference optimization framework represents a novel approach in this field.  Specifically, the automated generation of preference rankings is an important contribution to the methodology. This automates a step that is typically manually intensive in LLM preference optimization.

* **Significance:**  Inconsistent trajectory predictions can lead to unsafe planning and potentially dangerous situations for autonomous vehicles. By reducing inconsistencies (specifically collisions), the paper addresses a crucial challenge in deploying autonomous driving systems. The fact that the method can be applied as a fine-tuning step on existing models (both marginal and joint), without substantially impacting inference time, makes it practically significant. The demonstrated improvement in multiple datasets and models further strengthens its importance.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the problem of inconsistent predictions in multi-agent trajectory forecasting.
    * **Well-Motivated Approach:** The analogy between LLMs and trajectory prediction models, particularly the stochastic nature of predictions and the possibility of automatic ranking of bad predictions, is well explained and justifies the adaptation of preference optimization.
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experiments are conducted on several datasets with different state-of-the-art models, demonstrating the generality of the approach.  The inclusion of metrics beyond just collision rates (pSCR, MinJointFDE) provides a holistic evaluation. Ablation studies are also included to demonstrate the importance of hyperparameters.
    * **Practicality:** The method is presented as a fine-tuning strategy, which allows it to be easily integrated with existing prediction pipelines without significant overhead. The ability to improve collision rates in both marginal and joint prediction models is significant.
    * **Automated Ranking:** The use of an automated cost function to rank predictions avoids expensive manual labeling.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Theoretical Justification:** While the experimental results are convincing, the paper could benefit from a more rigorous theoretical justification for why preference optimization works well in this context.  Why is SimPO specifically appropriate for trajectory prediction?
    * **Trade-off between accuracy and consistency:** The authors mention a trade-off between accuracy and consistency. While the degradations in accuracy are small, a deeper analysis into why this tradeoff exists and how it might be mitigated would be valuable.
    * **Mode collapse:** Although acknowledged, the paper does not propose any solutions to mitigate the mode collapse problem in detail.
    * **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:**  The approach introduces several hyperparameters (β, γ, λ), and the ablation studies offer some guidance, but a more in-depth sensitivity analysis and strategies for tuning these parameters in new datasets would improve the practicality of the method.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by:
    * Encouraging researchers to explore the application of preference optimization techniques to other areas of robotics and autonomous systems.
    * Providing a practical method for improving the safety and reliability of trajectory prediction systems.
    * Promoting the development of more consistent and interpretable trajectory prediction models.

Overall, the paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of trajectory prediction. While some weaknesses exist, the strengths of the paper outweigh them. The demonstrated improvement in scene consistency, coupled with the method's practicality and broad applicability, makes it a valuable contribution.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### AvatarMakeup: Realistic Makeup Transfer for 3D Animatable Head Avatars
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02419v1)
- **Authors**: Yiming Zhong, Xiaolin Zhang, Ligang Liu, Yao Zhao, Yunchao Wei
- **Abstract**: Similar to facial beautification in real life, 3D virtual avatars require personalized customization to enhance their visual appeal, yet this area remains insufficiently explored. Although current 3D Gaussian editing methods can be adapted for facial makeup purposes, these methods fail to meet the fundamental requirements for achieving realistic makeup effects: 1) ensuring a consistent appearance during drivable expressions, 2) preserving the identity throughout the makeup process, and 3) enabling precise control over fine details. To address these, we propose a specialized 3D makeup method named AvatarMakeup, leveraging a pretrained diffusion model to transfer makeup patterns from a single reference photo of any individual. We adopt a coarse-to-fine idea to first maintain the consistent appearance and identity, and then to refine the details. In particular, the diffusion model is employed to generate makeup images as supervision. Due to the uncertainties in diffusion process, the generated images are inconsistent across different viewpoints and expressions. Therefore, we propose a Coherent Duplication method to coarsely apply makeup to the target while ensuring consistency across dynamic and multiview effects. Coherent Duplication optimizes a global UV map by recoding the averaged facial attributes among the generated makeup images. By querying the global UV map, it easily synthesizes coherent makeup guidance from arbitrary views and expressions to optimize the target avatar. Given the coarse makeup avatar, we further enhance the makeup by incorporating a Refinement Module into the diffusion model to achieve high makeup quality. Experiments demonstrate that AvatarMakeup achieves state-of-the-art makeup transfer quality and consistency throughout animation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "AvatarMakeup: Realistic Makeup Transfer for 3D Animatable Head Avatars":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AvatarMakeup, a novel framework for transferring makeup styles from a reference image to 3D animatable head avatars represented as Gaussian splattings. The core idea is to address the challenges of achieving realistic and consistent makeup effects on dynamic 3D avatars while preserving identity. AvatarMakeup utilizes a coarse-to-fine approach. It leverages a pre-trained diffusion model (Stable-Makeup) for initial makeup generation, addresses inconsistencies across different views and expressions with a "Coherent Duplication" method based on a global UV map, and then refines the makeup details using a Refinement Module integrated into the diffusion process.  The Coherent Duplication uses the mesh to create a global UV map that records the facial patterns for consistent representation across diverse poses and expressions.  The Refinement Module is added to increase detail and consistency by adding noise at small timestamps during the diffusion process. The authors demonstrate through experiments that AvatarMakeup achieves state-of-the-art makeup transfer quality and consistency throughout animation, outperforming existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper has reasonable novelty, primarily in its integration of existing techniques (Gaussian Splatting, diffusion models) to solve a specific problem in the context of 3D avatar customization. While existing methods can edit 3D models, they don't adequately address the combination of animatability, identity preservation, and fine-grained control required for realistic makeup transfer. The "Coherent Duplication" method and the Refinement Module are the most novel aspects, targeting specific limitations of directly applying image-based makeup transfer to 3D avatars. The novelty can be questioned, however, because it leverages existing diffusion models and simply adapts existing frameworks from 2D to 3D using UV maps which are well known concepts in the graphics community.

*   **Significance:** The significance is moderate. The demand for personalized avatar customization is growing, making this a relevant research area. Realistic makeup transfer enhances the visual appeal and realism of virtual avatars, which has applications in virtual reality, gaming, and other immersive environments. The main contribution comes from taking a framework from 2D makeup transfer and showing how to extend it to the 3D case.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Problem Definition:** The paper clearly defines the problem of realistic makeup transfer for 3D animatable avatars and identifies the key challenges: consistency, identity preservation, and precise control.
    *   **Technical Approach:** The coarse-to-fine strategy is well-motivated and addresses the identified challenges effectively. The Coherent Duplication method is a clever way to ensure consistency across different views and expressions.
    *   **Experimental Evaluation:** The paper presents comprehensive experimental results with quantitative and qualitative comparisons against baseline methods, demonstrating the superiority of AvatarMakeup.  The metrics are relevant and appropriate.  The ablation study effectively demonstrates the contribution of each component of the framework.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is generally well-written and easy to understand, with clear explanations of the proposed methods and experimental results.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Existing Techniques:** The method heavily relies on existing techniques like Gaussian Splatting, Stable-Makeup, and diffusion models. While the integration is novel, the individual components are not groundbreaking.
    *   **UV Map Limitations:** UV maps can introduce artifacts if the parameterization is not good, and don't encode fine details. How well this works across significantly different meshes might be a question to explore further.
    *   **Limited Scope:** The paper focuses primarily on head avatars. Extending the method to full-body avatars or more complex scenarios might present additional challenges.
    *   **Lack of Insight into Failure Cases:** While the paper shows successful examples, it doesn't deeply analyze the failure cases of the method or discuss the limitations in detail.

*   **Potential Influence:** AvatarMakeup has the potential to influence the field of 3D avatar customization by providing a practical and effective solution for realistic makeup transfer.  Other researchers can build upon this framework to explore more advanced makeup styles, personalized customization options, and integration with other avatar editing tools.

**Justification of Score:**

Based on the above analysis, I assign a score of 6.5. The paper addresses a relevant problem with a reasonably novel solution, demonstrating good experimental results. However, the reliance on existing techniques and certain limitations in scope prevent it from being a truly groundbreaking contribution. It's a solid engineering paper that pushes the state of the art but does not introduce fundamental breakthroughs.

**Score: 6.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Toward a Robust and Generalizable Metamaterial Foundation Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02436v1)
- **Authors**: Namjung Kim, Dongseok Lee, Jongbin Yu, Sung Woong Cho, Dosung Lee, Yesol Park, Youngjoon Hong
- **Abstract**: Advances in material functionalities drive innovations across various fields, where metamaterials-defined by structure rather than composition-are leading the way. Despite the rise of artificial intelligence (AI)-driven design strategies, their impact is limited by task-specific retraining, poor out-of-distribution(OOD) generalization, and the need for separate models for forward and inverse design. To address these limitations, we introduce the Metamaterial Foundation Model (MetaFO), a Bayesian transformer-based foundation model inspired by large language models. MetaFO learns the underlying mechanics of metamaterials, enabling probabilistic, zero-shot predictions across diverse, unseen combinations of material properties and structural responses. It also excels in nonlinear inverse design, even under OOD conditions. By treating metamaterials as an operator that maps material properties to structural responses, MetaFO uncovers intricate structure-property relationships and significantly expands the design space. This scalable and generalizable framework marks a paradigm shift in AI-driven metamaterial discovery, paving the way for next-generation innovations.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary, followed by a critical evaluation and a score for the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MetaFO, a Metamaterial Foundation Model, designed to address limitations in AI-driven metamaterial design. These limitations include the need for task-specific retraining, poor out-of-distribution (OOD) generalization, and separate models for forward and inverse design. MetaFO, inspired by large language models (LLMs) and Bayesian Transformers, learns underlying metamaterial mechanics, enabling probabilistic, zero-shot predictions across diverse material properties and structural responses, even in nonlinear inverse design and OOD scenarios. It unifies forward and inverse design tasks within a single operator-based framework and demonstrates robustness to noisy and incomplete data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novelty:** The application of foundation models, particularly Bayesian Transformers with prior-data fitting networks, to the domain of metamaterial design appears to be a significant step forward. The idea of treating metamaterials as operators and learning a generalized mapping between structure and properties is innovative.
    *   **Generalization and Robustness:** The paper emphasizes MetaFO's capacity for zero-shot learning, OOD generalization, noise reduction, and handling missing data. The experimental results seem to support these claims, demonstrating the model's adaptability to unseen configurations and materials.
    *   **Unification of Design Tasks:** The single framework for both forward and inverse design streamlines the design process and overcomes a major limitation of many existing approaches.
    *   **Performance:** The paper presents promising quantitative results, demonstrating that MetaFO outperforms existing ML models in various tasks.
    *   **Addressing a Real Problem:** Current data-driven metamaterial designs are often narrow and brittle, requiring substantial retraining for new tasks or datasets. MetaFO directly addresses this challenge.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Experimental Validation:** While the paper presents compelling results, a more thorough experimental validation with a broader range of metamaterial structures and real-world experimental data would strengthen the claims. The current study seems mostly based on simulation data.
    *   **Computational Cost and Scalability:** The computational cost of training and deploying a Bayesian Transformer-based foundation model can be significant. The paper does not extensively discuss the computational resources required, which are a practical consideration for wider adoption. The scalability to more complex and higher-dimensional metamaterial design problems should be further analyzed.
    *   **Prior Knowledge Dependency:** While leveraging prior knowledge from metamaterial databases is beneficial, it may also introduce biases and limit the model's ability to discover truly novel designs outside of existing datasets. The extent of this dependency is not clearly elucidated.
    *   **Overclaim:** The term "paradigm shift" might be an overclaim. While the paper introduces valuable advances, it remains to be seen if MetaFO will truly revolutionize the field in the long term. A more cautious assessment of the potential impact would be appropriate.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The paper provides access to the code to reproduce results, but the specific hyperparameters and configurations used may not be fully detailed, potentially hindering independent verification.
    *   **Clarity**: Although the idea is clearly communicated in the abstract, some detailed parts of the Methods can be difficult to grasp fully, which might affect reproducibility.

*   **Significance:**
    *   MetaFO can drastically reduce the resources necessary for metamaterial development and optimization by avoiding computationally expensive simulations.
    *   MetaFO has the potential to automate metamaterial discovery, allowing designers to identify promising structures for further exploration.

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to metamaterial design using foundation models. The demonstrated ability to generalize across different materials and structures, handle noisy data, and unify forward and inverse design is significant. However, the limitations related to computational cost, experimental validation (reliance on simulation), and the extent of prior knowledge dependency detract from its potential impact. Furthermore, the "paradigm shift" description seems overly enthusiastic at this point. Therefore, the paper scores above average in novelty and significance.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: physics.optics
- **Score**: 7/10

### System-performance and cost modeling of Large Language Model training and inference
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02456v1)
