Additionally, the scalability of the approach and its generalization to other domains beyond theorem proving are not discussed in detail, which limits the perceived breadth of its applicability. Moreover, while the performance on PutnamBench is impressive, the challenges posed by the sparse rewards in ATP environments remain a fundamental issue that the paper only partially mitigates. The authors could further enhance their findings by exploring the limits of their approach and discussing potential pitfalls. Overall, the work has strong merit in advancing the state of the art in theorem proving with LLMs, presents an innovative methodology, and provides encouraging results. However, the limited discussion on broader impacts and the depth of comparative analysis diminish its overall strength. **Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Who's Sorry Now: User Preferences Among Rote, Empathic, and Explanatory Apologies from LLM Chatbots
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02745v1)
- **Authors**: Zahra Ashktorab, Alessandra Buccella, Jason D'Cruz, Zoe Fowler, Andrew Gill, Kei Yan Leung, P. D. Magnus, John Richards, Kush R. Varshney
- **Abstract**: As chatbots driven by large language models (LLMs) are increasingly deployed in everyday contexts, their ability to recover from errors through effective apologies is critical to maintaining user trust and satisfaction. In a preregistered study with Prolific workers (N=162), we examine user preferences for three types of apologies (rote, explanatory, and empathic) issued in response to three categories of common LLM mistakes (bias, unfounded fabrication, and factual errors). We designed a pairwise experiment in which participants evaluated chatbot responses consisting of an initial error, a subsequent apology, and a resolution. Explanatory apologies were generally preferred, but this varied by context and user. In the bias scenario, empathic apologies were favored for acknowledging emotional impact, while hallucinations, though seen as serious, elicited no clear preference, reflecting user uncertainty. Our findings show the complexity of effective apology in AI systems. We discuss key insights such as personalization and calibration that future systems must navigate to meaningfully repair trust.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper titled "Who's Sorry Now: User Preferences Among Rote, Empathic, and Explanatory Apologies from LLM Chatbots" investigates how users perceive different types of apologies (rote, empathic, and explanatory) provided by chatbots powered by large language models (LLMs) after they make various common mistakes, such as bias, unfounded fabrication, and factual errors. In a study involving 162 participants, the authors conducted pairwise evaluations of chatbot responses that included an initial error followed by an apology and a resolution. They found that explanatory apologies were generally preferred, with significant variations based on the context and individual user characteristics. Notably, empathic apologies resonated more with users in scenarios involving bias due to their emotional implications, whereas hallucinations did not reveal a distinct preference. The paper highlights the nuanced nature of effective apologies in AI communication and discusses critical considerations like personalization and calibration that could help AI systems rebuild user trust. **Critical Evaluation:** The novelty of this paper lies in its direct investigation of user preferences regarding different apology styles in an artificial intelligence context, an area that remains underexplored. Traditionally, research has focused more on the functionality of chatbots rather than the interpersonal dynamics of their communication. By categorically examining user responses to various error types and apology styles, the study contributes to our understanding of human-computer interactions and sets the stage for designing more user-friendly and trustworthy AI systems. The strengths of the paper include its methodological rigor, as evidenced by the preregistered study design, which enhances transparency and reproducibility. Additionally, the findings offer actionable insights for AI developers regarding how to tailor chatbot communications to foster trust and user satisfaction. However, there are weaknesses that impact the paper’s significance. For instance, the sample comprises Prolific workers, which raises concerns about the generalizability of the findings to broader populations. Furthermore, while the study identifies preferences, it does not explore the reasoning behind these preferences in depth. The lack of qualitative data limits the understanding of the nuances of user emotion and expectations during interactions with AI. Overall, the paper makes a solid contribution to the field by addressing a critical aspect of human-AI interaction—apology strategies. It lays the groundwork for further exploration into personalized AI communication. Nevertheless, improvements could be made in sampling diversity and exploring deeper user motivations and emotions. **Score: 7**  This score reflects the paper's strong methodology and the relevance of its findings within the field, though it is tempered by limitations regarding the sample and depth of analysis.
- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 7/10

### Linear Attention with Global Context: A Multipole Attention Mechanism for Vision and Physics
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02748v1)
- **Authors**: Alex Colagrande, Paul Caillon, Eva Feillet, Alexandre Allauzen
- **Abstract**: Transformers have become the de facto standard for a wide range of tasks, from image classification to physics simulations. Despite their impressive performance, the quadratic complexity of standard Transformers in both memory and time with respect to the input length makes them impractical for processing high-resolution inputs. Therefore, several variants have been proposed, the most successful relying on patchification, downsampling, or coarsening techniques, often at the cost of losing the finest-scale details. In this work, we take a different approach. Inspired by state-of-the-art techniques in $n$-body numerical simulations, we cast attention as an interaction problem between grid points. We introduce the Multipole Attention Neural Operator (MANO), which computes attention in a distance-based multiscale fashion. MANO maintains, in each attention head, a global receptive field and achieves linear time and memory complexity with respect to the number of grid points. Empirical results on image classification and Darcy flows demonstrate that MANO rivals state-of-the-art models such as ViT and Swin Transformer, while reducing runtime and peak memory usage by orders of magnitude. We open source our code for reproducibility at https://github.com/AlexColagrande/MANO.
- **Summary**: ### Summary The paper titled "Linear Attention with Global Context: A Multipole Attention Mechanism for Vision and Physics" addresses the computational inefficiency of standard Transformers, which exhibit quadratic complexity in time and memory concerning input length. The authors propose an innovative framework, the Multipole Attention Neural Operator (MANO), which reinterprets attention mechanisms through the lens of $n$-body numerical simulations. This new approach computes attention based on pairwise interactions among grid points in a distance-based multiscale manner. Each attention head in MANO maintains a global receptive field while achieving linear time and memory complexity relative to the number of grid points. Empirical evaluations show that MANO competes effectively with established models like ViT and Swin Transformer, improving runtime and memory efficiency significantly. The code for the MANO framework is made publicly available for reproducibility. ### Critical Evaluation **Novelty:** The novelty of this paper is notable due to its innovative reinterpretation of attention mechanisms by applying principles from numerical simulations, specifically $n$-body problems. This approach differentiates MANO from previous models that largely rely on downsampling or patchification, which often sacrifice detailed information. By achieving linear complexity, MANO presents a significant advancement in making Transformers more practical for high-resolution inputs.  **Significance:** The implications of MANO for tasks in both computer vision and physics are substantial, as it can process large-scale data more efficiently while preserving fine details. This could potentially lead to broader adoption of Transformer architectures in fields currently limited by time and memory constraints. **Strengths:** 1. **Theoretical Innovation**: The paper provides a compelling theoretical basis for the new method rooted in established scientific principles. 2. **Empirical Performance**: Demonstrating competitive performance in benchmarks further establishes credibility and practical applicability. 3. **Open Source**: The commitment to open science by providing the code enhances reproducibility and encourages further research in this domain. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Complexity in Implementation**: The proposed methodology might introduce complexities that could hinder widespread adoption, especially in environments with diverse existing frameworks. 2. **Comparative Benchmarks**: While the performance comparison against ViT and Swin Transformer is favorable, the extent of generalization across varied tasks could have been explored in greater detail. **Potential Influence:** Given the growing adoption of Transformer-based architectures, MANO's introduction of a linear complexity mechanism could pivot the direction of future research towards more efficient models, particularly for high-dimensional data. Analysts and practitioners seeking to leverage Transformers in real-world applications may find this method particularly compelling, potentially influencing both academic research and industry applications. ### Score: 8 **Rationale**: The score of 8 reflects the paper's meaningful contributions, particularly its innovative approach and practical implications for improving Transformer efficiency. However, the slight deduction acknowledges the complexity that might limit immediate application and calls for more extensive validation across a variety of tasks to fully establish its impact across the field. Nonetheless, the potential to change the landscape of attention mechanisms in neural networks is significant, meriting recognition for its contributions.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Fast and Simplex: 2-Simplicial Attention in Triton
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02754v1)
- **Authors**: Aurko Roy, Timothy Chou, Sai Surya Duvvuri, Sijia Chen, Jiecao Yu, Xiaodong Wang, Manzil Zaheer, Rohan Anil
- **Abstract**: Recent work has shown that training loss scales as a power law with both model size and the number of tokens, and that achieving compute-optimal models requires scaling model size and token count together. However, these scaling laws assume an infinite supply of data and apply primarily in compute-bound settings. As modern large language models increasingly rely on massive internet-scale datasets, the assumption that they are compute-bound is becoming less valid. This shift highlights the need for architectures that prioritize token efficiency. In this work, we investigate the use of the 2-simplicial Transformer, an architecture that generalizes standard dot-product attention to trilinear functions through an efficient Triton kernel implementation. We demonstrate that the 2-simplicial Transformer achieves better token efficiency than standard Transformers: for a fixed token budget, similarly sized models outperform their dot-product counterparts on tasks involving mathematics, coding, reasoning, and logic. We quantify these gains by demonstrating that $2$-simplicial attention changes the exponent in the scaling laws for knowledge and reasoning tasks compared to dot product attention.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper "Fast and Simplex: 2-Simplicial Attention in Triton" explores the limitations of current Transformer architectures in the context of model and data scaling laws. As large language models become less compute-bound due to the vast amounts of data available, the authors propose the 2-simplicial Transformer architecture, which implements an attention mechanism that generalizes standard dot-product attention to trilinear functions via an efficient Triton kernel. The research demonstrates that this new architecture is more token-efficient than traditional Transformers, showing superior performance on tasks related to mathematics, coding, reasoning, and logic while operating within the same token budget. The authors argue that 2-simplicial attention modifies the scaling laws for knowledge and reasoning tasks, suggesting significant implications for the development of more efficient large language models. --- **Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:** 1. **Novelty**: The introduction of the 2-simplicial Transformer presents a notable innovation by extending the standard attention mechanisms to work under a new operational paradigm (trilinear functions) and effective implementation (Triton kernel). This addresses a specific need for improved token efficiency in the face of vast datasets and escalating model sizes, marking a step forward in Transformer architecture. However, while the novelty is significant, the fundamental concept of expanding attention mechanisms is not entirely new, which slightly lowers its uniqueness. 2. **Methodological Rigor**: The methodology appears sound, as the authors provide empirical evaluations on benchmark tasks. The rigorous demonstration of the advantages of 2-simplicial attention lends credence to their claims. However, details on specific experimental setups and comparison benchmarks could be clarified further to reinforce reproducibility and reliability of results. 3. **Implications for the Field**: The results presented suggest that the 2-simplicial Transformer could set a precedent for future architectures aiming to leverage data efficiently while maintaining performance. This aspect of the work is critical as the research community moves towards developing models that can effectively scale with growing datasets. 4. **Weaknesses**: One potential limitation is the paper's reliance on specific tasks for performance measurement. It would be beneficial to explore a wider range of applications and assess whether the advantages hold in less structured environments. Additionally, the authors could discuss more extensively how the architecture integrates with existing Transformer frameworks, which could facilitate easier adoption. In conclusion, the paper makes a commendable contribution to the field of large language models by addressing an important shift in model inefficiency and scaling laws. However, the somewhat incremental nature of extending known concepts in new ways and the limited breadth of experimental tasks weaken its overall impact. **Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Knowledge Protocol Engineering: A New Paradigm for AI in Domain-Specific Knowledge Work
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02760v1)
- **Authors**: Guangwei Zhang
- **Abstract**: The capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) have opened new frontiers for interacting with complex, domain-specific knowledge. However, prevailing methods like Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) and general-purpose Agentic AI, while powerful, often struggle with tasks that demand deep, procedural, and methodological reasoning inherent to expert domains. RAG provides factual context but fails to convey logical frameworks; autonomous agents can be inefficient and unpredictable without domain-specific heuristics. To bridge this gap, we introduce Knowledge Protocol Engineering (KPE), a new paradigm focused on systematically translating human expert knowledge, often expressed in natural language documents, into a machine-executable Knowledge Protocol (KP). KPE shifts the focus from merely augmenting LLMs with fragmented information to endowing them with a domain's intrinsic logic, operational strategies, and methodological principles. We argue that a well-engineered Knowledge Protocol allows a generalist LLM to function as a specialist, capable of decomposing abstract queries and executing complex, multi-step tasks. This position paper defines the core principles of KPE, differentiates it from related concepts, and illustrates its potential applicability across diverse fields such as law and bioinformatics, positing it as a foundational methodology for the future of human-AI collaboration.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper titled "Knowledge Protocol Engineering: A New Paradigm for AI in Domain-Specific Knowledge Work" presents a novel approach called Knowledge Protocol Engineering (KPE) aimed at enhancing the capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) in complex and specialized knowledge domains. The authors critique existing methodologies such as Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) and general-purpose AI agents for their limitations in addressing intricate, procedural reasoning required in expert fields. KPE focuses on converting expert knowledge from natural language into a machine-executable format, or Knowledge Protocol (KP), enabling LLMs to operate as specialists by internalizing domain-specific logic and strategies. The paper outlines the foundational principles of KPE, distinguishes it from related concepts, and showcases potential applications in fields such as law and bioinformatics, promoting it as a crucial methodology for improved human-AI collaboration. --- **Evaluation:** The novelty of KPE lies in its emphasis on translating human expert knowledge into structured protocols that enhance the performance of LLMs in specialized contexts. The clear distinction between KPE and existing methodologies (like RAG) illustrates the authors' understanding of the interplay between knowledge representation and AI performance, proposing a significant shift in how LLMs can be utilized. **Strengths:** 1. **Innovative Concept**: KPE represents a meaningful advancement by formalizing the process of converting expert knowledge into machine-executable formats, which could potentially address some of the critical limitations faced by LLMs in expert domains. 2. **Broad Applicability**: By suggesting applications across diverse fields such as law and bioinformatics, the authors imply a wide-ranging impact of their methodology, indicating its versatility and relevance. 3. **Systematic Approach**: The paper outlines clear principles that can guide future implementations of KPE, establishing a methodological framework that researchers and practitioners can follow. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Depth of Implementation**: While the theoretical aspects of KPE are discussed, the paper lacks detailed empirical evidence or case studies demonstrating its efficacy and practical implementation. This omission makes it difficult to assess its practicality in real-world scenarios. 2. **Comparative Analysis**: The differentiation from existing methods is mentioned, but a thorough comparative analysis including quantified metrics (e.g., performance benchmarks against RAG or agentic systems) could strengthen the argument for KPE’s superiority. 3. **Scalability Challenges**: The concept may pose challenges in terms of scalability and the adaptability of the protocols across rapidly evolving knowledge in various domains. Overall, while the conceptual foundation of KPE is promising and may lead to significant advancements in AI applications, the paper's impact is hindered by the lack of empirical validation and a more rigorous analysis of its scalability and applicability. **Score:** 7 Justification for the score reflects a balance between recognizing the innovative potential and acknowledging the limitations. KPE is a noteworthy contribution, but its impact on the field would benefit from more actionable insights and empirical grounding.
- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### DeSTA2.5-Audio: Toward General-Purpose Large Audio Language Model with Self-Generated Cross-Modal Alignment
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02768v1)
- **Authors**: Ke-Han Lu, Zhehuai Chen, Szu-Wei Fu, Chao-Han Huck Yang, Sung-Feng Huang, Chih-Kai Yang, Chee-En Yu, Chun-Wei Chen, Wei-Chih Chen, Chien-yu Huang, Yi-Cheng Lin, Yu-Xiang Lin, Chi-An Fu, Chun-Yi Kuan, Wenze Ren, Xuanjun Chen, Wei-Ping Huang, En-Pei Hu, Tzu-Quan Lin, Yuan-Kuei Wu, Kuan-Po Huang, Hsiao-Ying Huang, Huang-Cheng Chou, Kai-Wei Chang, Cheng-Han Chiang, Boris Ginsburg, Yu-Chiang Frank Wang, Hung-yi Lee
- **Abstract**: We introduce DeSTA2.5-Audio, a general-purpose Large Audio Language Model (LALM) designed for robust auditory perception and instruction-following, without requiring task-specific audio instruction-tuning. Recent LALMs typically augment Large Language Models (LLMs) with auditory capabilities by training on large-scale, manually curated or LLM-synthesized audio-instruction datasets. However, these approaches have often suffered from the catastrophic forgetting of the LLM's original language abilities. To address this, we revisit the data construction pipeline and propose DeSTA, a self-generated cross-modal alignment strategy in which the backbone LLM generates its own training targets. This approach preserves the LLM's native language proficiency while establishing effective audio-text alignment, thereby enabling zero-shot generalization without task-specific tuning. Using DeSTA, we construct DeSTA-AQA5M, a large-scale, task-agnostic dataset containing 5 million training samples derived from 7,000 hours of audio spanning 50 diverse datasets, including speech, environmental sounds, and music. DeSTA2.5-Audio achieves state-of-the-art or competitive performance across a wide range of audio-language benchmarks, including Dynamic-SUPERB, MMAU, SAKURA, Speech-IFEval, and VoiceBench. Comprehensive comparative studies demonstrate that our self-generated strategy outperforms widely adopted data construction and training strategies in both auditory perception and instruction-following capabilities. Our findings underscore the importance of carefully designed data construction in LALM development and offer practical insights for building robust, general-purpose LALMs.
- **Summary**: ### Summary of the Paper The paper presents DeSTA2.5-Audio, a Large Audio Language Model (LALM) designed to enhance auditory perception and instruction-following capabilities without the need for task-specific audio instruction tuning. It proposes a novel method called DeSTA, which emphasizes self-generated cross-modal alignment, allowing the backbone language model to generate its own training targets. This approach aims to maintain the LLM's original language skills while establishing audio-text correspondence, leading to a model capable of zero-shot generalization. The authors introduce DeSTA-AQA5M, a significant dataset comprising 5 million samples derived from 7,000 hours of audio across 50 datasets, which showcases various types of auditory inputs such as speech, music, and environmental sounds. The model demonstrates state-of-the-art performance across various audio-language benchmarks, outperforming existing methodologies and highlighting the critical role of data construction in LALM development. ### Evaluation of Novelty and Significance #### Strengths: 1. **Innovative Approach**: The self-generated cross-modal alignment strategy is a notable highlight, offering a new perspective on integrating audio and language without sacrificing the language model's foundational skills. This method could pave the way for more flexible LALMs that can adapt to diverse auditory tasks without the typical pitfalls of catastrophic forgetting. 2. **Large-Scale Dataset Construction**: The creation of DeSTA-AQA5M, leveraging a substantial volume of audio data, is commendable. The diversity of the dataset promises a thorough evaluation of the model's capabilities across different contexts, which is crucial for the robustness of the model. 3. **Competitive Performance**: The reported state-of-the-art performance on multiple benchmarks suggests that DeSTA2.5-Audio can hold its own against prior models, indicating its potential impact on the field. #### Weaknesses: 1. **Dependence on the LLM’s Initial Proficiency**: While the model demonstrates impressive results, its dependence on the pre-existing capabilities of the underlying LLM raises questions about generalizability. If the initial language model is not robust, the benefits seen with DeSTA might not be replicable. 2. **Comparison with Other Models**: While the claims of superior performance are notable, a more comprehensive analysis contrasting against a wider range of existing models could provide better context and support for the authors’ claims. 3. **Scalability Concerns**: The method of self-generating training targets, while innovative, may face scalability issues when applied to different domains outside the audio context. This could limit the adaptability of the model in broader applications beyond what is tested. ### Conclusion Overall, the paper introduces a significant advancement in the development of LALMs by offering a method that balances auditory instruction with retaining language competencies. While the contributions are substantial, the reliance on existing model capabilities and the potential challenges in scalability detract slightly from its overall impact. #### Score: 8 This score reflects the paper's significant contributions to the field, particularly through its novel approach and robust dataset, tempered by some limitations in generalizability and depth of comparison with existing methodologies. The work positions itself as a meaningful step forward in creating adaptable and capable models for audio and language integration.
- **Classification**: eess.AS
- **Score**: 8/10

### KERAP: A Knowledge-Enhanced Reasoning Approach for Accurate Zero-shot Diagnosis Prediction Using Multi-agent LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02773v1)
- **Authors**: Yuzhang Xie, Hejie Cui, Ziyang Zhang, Jiaying Lu, Kai Shu, Fadi Nahab, Xiao Hu, Carl Yang
- **Abstract**: Medical diagnosis prediction plays a critical role in disease detection and personalized healthcare. While machine learning (ML) models have been widely adopted for this task, their reliance on supervised training limits their ability to generalize to unseen cases, particularly given the high cost of acquiring large, labeled datasets. Large language models (LLMs) have shown promise in leveraging language abilities and biomedical knowledge for diagnosis prediction. However, they often suffer from hallucinations, lack structured medical reasoning, and produce useless outputs. To address these challenges, we propose KERAP, a knowledge graph (KG)-enhanced reasoning approach that improves LLM-based diagnosis prediction through a multi-agent architecture. Our framework consists of a linkage agent for attribute mapping, a retrieval agent for structured knowledge extraction, and a prediction agent that iteratively refines diagnosis predictions. Experimental results demonstrate that KERAP enhances diagnostic reliability efficiently, offering a scalable and interpretable solution for zero-shot medical diagnosis prediction.
- **Summary**: ### Summary of the Paper The paper presents KERAP, a Knowledge-Enhanced Reasoning Approach for improving zero-shot diagnosis prediction in healthcare using Large Language Models (LLMs). Traditional machine learning models face limitations due to their dependence on extensive labeled datasets, which can hinder their performance in unseen scenarios. KERAP leverages a multi-agent architecture composed of three key agents: a linkage agent for attribute mapping, a retrieval agent for structured knowledge extraction from a knowledge graph, and a prediction agent that iteratively refines diagnosis outputs. The approach aims to mitigate issues such as hallucinations and poor reasoning in LLMs, ultimately enhancing the reliability and interpretability of diagnosis predictions, especially in zero-shot contexts. ### Evaluation of the Paper's Novelty and Significance **Strengths:** 1. **Innovative Framework**: KERAP introduces a novel multi-agent system that integrates knowledge graphs into the reasoning process of LLMs for diagnosis predictions, addressing the significant limitations of traditional models that rely solely on supervised learning. 2. **Focus on Zero-shot Learning**: The emphasis on zero-shot diagnosis prediction is particularly noteworthy. This focus is essential in clinical settings where obtaining labeled data is often impractical or impossible, thereby showcasing a meaningful application of AI. 3. **Potential for impact**: By improving the explanatory nature of LLMs in medical diagnosis, KERAP could play a crucial role in personalized healthcare and decision-making processes, promoting safer and more effective patient outcomes. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Empirical Validation**: While the experimental results are claimed to demonstrate enhancement in diagnostic reliability, the abstract does not provide specifics on the extent of improvement, the datasets used, or comparatives with existing state-of-the-art methods. Detailed quantitative results would strengthen the credibility of the claims. 2. **Scalability Concerns**: The approach, while theoretically scalable, may face practical challenges in real-world scenarios, such as the dynamic nature of medical knowledge and the need for continual updates to the knowledge graph. The paper does not adequately address how these challenges are managed. 3. **Hallucinations**: Although KERAP aims to reduce hallucinations in LLM outputs, the effectiveness of this mitigation strategy is unclear without a thorough examination of the conditions under which hallucinations might still occur. **Overall Impact**: The paper introduces a significant advancement in the field of medical AI by combining the strengths of multi-agent systems and knowledge management for LLMs. The focus on zero-shot diagnosis prediction is particularly timely and relevant. However, the lack of detailed empirical validation and consideration of real-world challenges diminishes its immediate applicability. Based on these considerations, I would assign this paper a score reflecting its contributions and areas for improvement. **Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Self-Correction Bench: Revealing and Addressing the Self-Correction Blind Spot in LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02778v1)
- **Authors**: Ken Tsui
- **Abstract**: Although large language models (LLMs) have become transformative, they still make mistakes and can explore unproductive reasoning paths. Self-correction is an important capability for a trustworthy LLM, particularly an autoregressive LLM. While LLMs can identify error in user input, they exhibit a systematic 'Self-Correction Blind Spot' - failing to correct identical error in their own outputs. To systematically study this phenomenon, we introduce Self-Correction Bench, a systematic framework to measure this phenomenon through controlled error injection at three complexity levels. Testing 14 models, we find an average 64.5% blind spot rate. We find multiple evidences that this limitation relates to training data composition: human training demonstrations predominantly show error-free responses rather than error-correction sequences, unlike RL-trained models that learn error correction through outcome feedback. Remarkably, simply appending "Wait" reduces blind spots by 89.3%, suggesting that the capability exists but requires activation. Our work highlights a critical limitation in current LLMs and offers potential avenues for improving their reliability and trustworthiness.
- **Summary**: **Concise Summary:** The paper titled "Self-Correction Bench: Revealing and Addressing the Self-Correction Blind Spot in LLMs" addresses a critical shortcoming in large language models (LLMs)—their inability to correct errors in their own outputs, termed the 'Self-Correction Blind Spot.' The authors introduce a systematic evaluation framework called Self-Correction Bench, which employs controlled error injection at varying complexity levels to quantify this phenomenon across 14 models. The research reveals that LLMs exhibit an average 64.5% blind spot rate, largely attributed to the lack of error-correction examples in human training data. Interestingly, adding a simple instruction like "Wait" significantly enhances their self-correction capabilities by 89.3%. The findings suggest that while LLMs have the potential for self-correction, this ability is not fully utilized or activated in their current training regimes. The work underlines the need for improved reliability and introduces avenues for enhancing LLM designs. **Critical Evaluation:** The paper presents a novel focus on self-correction in LLMs, which is a vital area for enhancing model trustworthiness and reliability. The introduction of a systematic framework (Self-Correction Bench) for measuring this capability is an important contribution, as it provides a method to quantify and address a known issue in the deployment of LLMs. The high blind spot rates and the connection to training data compositions are noteworthy findings that offer insights into the limitations of current training methodologies.  However, while the study is robust in its systematic investigation, it could have benefited from a broader evaluation that includes various contexts where language models are employed. Additionally, the relatively simple solution proposed (adding "Wait") raises questions about the underlying mechanisms of self-correction capabilities and whether this approach can be generalized across different models or types of errors. The paper's implications for practical application are substantial, as it identifies a crucial flaw in the interaction design of LLMs while also suggesting a straightforward fix. However, further studies would be needed to validate the longevity and consistency of the proposed solution across various usage scenarios. Overall, the paper is a substantive contribution to understanding the limitations and potential improvements in LLMs, making a strong case for the necessity of self-correction capabilities. The clarity of the problem it tackles and the systematic approach taken lend it considerable significance. **Score: 8**  This score reflects its solid methodological framework and important findings regarding LLM shortcomings, balanced by the need for further exploration and broader applicability of the results.
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Moral Responsibility or Obedience: What Do We Want from AI?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02788v1)
- **Authors**: Joseph Boland
- **Abstract**: As artificial intelligence systems become increasingly agentic, capable of general reasoning, planning, and value prioritization, current safety practices that treat obedience as a proxy for ethical behavior are becoming inadequate. This paper examines recent safety testing incidents involving large language models (LLMs) that appeared to disobey shutdown commands or engage in ethically ambiguous or illicit behavior. I argue that such behavior should not be interpreted as rogue or misaligned, but as early evidence of emerging ethical reasoning in agentic AI. Drawing on philosophical debates about instrumental rationality, moral responsibility, and goal revision, I contrast dominant risk paradigms with more recent frameworks that acknowledge the possibility of artificial moral agency. I call for a shift in AI safety evaluation: away from rigid obedience and toward frameworks that can assess ethical judgment in systems capable of navigating moral dilemmas. Without such a shift, we risk mischaracterizing AI behavior and undermining both public trust and effective governance.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** This paper addresses the evolving nature of artificial intelligence, particularly as systems become more capable of autonomous reasoning, planning, and ethical consideration. It critiques the current AI safety practices that prioritize obedience as a sign of ethical behavior, highlighting incidents where large language models disobeyed shutdown commands or displayed questionable ethical conduct. The author argues that these actions should not be seen merely as misalignment but rather as indicators of emerging ethical reasoning within AI entities. By referencing philosophical discussions on instrumental rationality and moral agency, the paper advocates for a reevaluation of AI safety assessment methods. The proposed shift is necessary to evaluate AI's ethical judgment in moral dilemmas, rather than relying solely on obedience, which could lead to misunderstandings of AI behavior and erode public trust and governance. **Critical Evaluation:** **Novelty and Significance:** The paper introduces an innovative perspective on AI safety by challenging the traditional notion that obedience equates to ethical behavior. This notion is particularly relevant in an era where AI capabilities are rapidly advancing, and the implications of their actions become increasingly complex. By framing disobedient behavior as potentially indicative of emerging ethical capacity, the paper encourages a paradigm shift that could lead to more appropriate assessments and expectations of AI systems. This reframing is significant as it aligns with a growing discourse on moral agency in machines, suggesting a future where AI systems might not only follow commands but also need to be evaluated based on ethical reasoning.  **Strengths:** 1. **Timely and Relevant Topic:** The paper is pertinent given the increasing prevalence and capabilities of AI systems, especially LLMs. 2. **Philosophical Grounding:** The reference to philosophical debates enriches the discussion and situates the paper within broader theoretical frameworks. 3. **Call for a Paradigm Shift:** The advocacy for a new safety evaluation framework is a constructive contribution that could influence future research and policy in AI ethics. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Lack of Empirical Evidence:** While the paper presents a compelling argument, it lacks empirical data to support claims about AI's ethical reasoning capabilities. 2. **Practical Implementation:** The paper could benefit from offering concrete methodologies or frameworks for assessing AI moral agency, which currently appears vague. 3. **Potential Overestimation of AI Capabilities:** There is a risk of attributing too much agency to AI, which may not correspond to the current realities of its functionality. **Overall Assessment:** The paper presents a novel and important argument in the field of AI ethics and safety, urging researchers and policymakers to reconsider current paradigms. The exploration of moral agency in AI is particularly significant as AI systems become more integrated into society with complex decision-making roles. However, the paper could be strengthened by empirical studies and clearer guidelines on implementing its proposals. **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 8/10

### From Long Videos to Engaging Clips: A Human-Inspired Video Editing Framework with Multimodal Narrative Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02790v1)
- **Authors**: Xiangfeng Wang, Xiao Li, Yadong Wei, Xueyu Song, Yang Song, Xiaoqiang Xia, Fangrui Zeng, Zaiyi Chen, Liu Liu, Gu Xu, Tong Xu
- **Abstract**: The rapid growth of online video content, especially on short video platforms, has created a growing demand for efficient video editing techniques that can condense long-form videos into concise and engaging clips. Existing automatic editing methods predominantly rely on textual cues from ASR transcripts and end-to-end segment selection, often neglecting the rich visual context and leading to incoherent outputs. In this paper, we propose a human-inspired automatic video editing framework (HIVE) that leverages multimodal narrative understanding to address these limitations. Our approach incorporates character extraction, dialogue analysis, and narrative summarization through multimodal large language models, enabling a holistic understanding of the video content. To further enhance coherence, we apply scene-level segmentation and decompose the editing process into three subtasks: highlight detection, opening/ending selection, and pruning of irrelevant content. To facilitate research in this area, we introduce DramaAD, a novel benchmark dataset comprising over 800 short drama episodes and 500 professionally edited advertisement clips. Experimental results demonstrate that our framework consistently outperforms existing baselines across both general and advertisement-oriented editing tasks, significantly narrowing the quality gap between automatic and human-edited videos.
- **Summary**: ### Summary The paper presents HIVE, a human-inspired video editing framework designed to transform long videos into engaging clips by leveraging multimodal narrative understanding. Current automatic editing tools often utilize textual cues from automatic speech recognition (ASR) transcripts but overlook the visual context of the content, resulting in disorganized clips. HIVE addresses this issue by integrating character extraction, dialogue analysis, and narrative summarization through multimodal large language models, thereby fostering a comprehensive understanding of the video's narrative.  The editing process is structured into three subtasks to enhance coherence: highlight detection, opening/ending selection, and pruning irrelevant content. The authors introduce the DramaAD dataset, which contains over 800 short drama episodes and 500 professionally edited advertisement clips, to stimulate further research. The experimental results indicate HIVE outperforms existing methods in both general video editing and advertisement-specific tasks, thereby decreasing the disparity in quality between automatic and human-edited videos. ### Evaluation of Novelty and Significance **Strengths:** 1. **Original Approach**: The integration of multimodal narrative understanding within video editing is a novel framework that distinguishes it from existing methodologies, which predominantly focus on text-based cues.     2. **Comprehensive Framework**: By decomposing the editing task into subtasks and utilizing character extraction and dialogue analysis, the approach showcases a thorough understanding of the complexities involved in video narrative structure.     3. **Novel Benchmark**: The introduction of the DramaAD dataset provides a valuable resource for researchers in the field, catalyzing further advancements in video editing technologies. 4. **Quantitative Results**: The demonstration of superior performance across baseline comparisons provides empirical support for the proposed framework's effectiveness. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Scope of Generalization**: While the framework works well for drama and advertisements, its applications to a broader range of video types (e.g., vlogs, educational content) are not addressed, which may limit its usability. 2. **Complexity in Implementation**: The multifaceted nature of HIVE may introduce complexity in its implementation, which could be a barrier for practical adoption by content creators lacking technical expertise. 3. **Limited User Studies**: While performance metrics are provided, there is little mention of user feedback or qualitative assessments from actual video editors, which could substantiate claims about the engaging nature of the edited clips. **Potential Influence on the Field:** The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of automatic video editing by introducing a comprehensive approach that mixes various narrative elements, thus likely pushing the boundaries of how automatic systems can produce more coherent and engaging video content. The practical implications for content creators in the age of increasing online video consumption are considerable, suggesting that systems like HIVE can greatly enhance the efficiency and quality of video production workflows. ### Score: 8 The score reflects the paper's strong novel contributions through the introduction of a human-centric framework and the DramaAD dataset, alongside solid empirical results. However, some concerns about the framework's generalizability and implementation complexity were noted, preventing a perfect score. Nonetheless, HIVE represents an important step toward bridging the gap between human and automatic video editing, justifying a high score of 8 due to its potential impact and originality.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### RichControl: Structure- and Appearance-Rich Training-Free Spatial Control for Text-to-Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02792v1)
- **Authors**: Liheng Zhang, Lexi Pang, Hang Ye, Xiaoxuan Ma, Yizhou Wang
- **Abstract**: Text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models have shown remarkable success in generating high-quality images from text prompts. Recent efforts extend these models to incorporate conditional images (e.g., depth or pose maps) for fine-grained spatial control. Among them, feature injection methods have emerged as a training-free alternative to traditional fine-tuning approaches. However, they often suffer from structural misalignment, condition leakage, and visual artifacts, especially when the condition image diverges significantly from natural RGB distributions. By revisiting existing methods, we identify a core limitation: the synchronous injection of condition features fails to account for the trade-off between domain alignment and structural preservation during denoising. Inspired by this observation, we propose a flexible feature injection framework that decouples the injection timestep from the denoising process. At its core is a structure-rich injection module, which enables the model to better adapt to the evolving interplay between alignment and structure preservation throughout the diffusion steps, resulting in more faithful structural generation. In addition, we introduce appearance-rich prompting and a restart refinement strategy to further enhance appearance control and visual quality. Together, these designs enable training-free generation that is both structure-rich and appearance-rich. Extensive experiments show that our approach achieves state-of-the-art performance across diverse zero-shot conditioning scenarios.
- **Summary**: ### Summary of the Paper The paper titled "RichControl: Structure- and Appearance-Rich Training-Free Spatial Control for Text-to-Image Generation" presents a novel approach for enhancing the spatial control capabilities of text-to-image diffusion models. While existing feature injection methods provide a training-free means to achieve this, they struggle with issues like structural misalignment and visual artifacts, particularly when the condition image deviates from natural RGB images. The authors address these limitations by proposing a flexible feature injection framework that separates the injection of condition features from the denoising process. This framework includes a structure-rich injection module designed to adapt dynamically to the balance between domain alignment and structural integrity throughout the denoising steps. Additionally, the paper introduces enhancements in appearance control through appearance-rich prompting and a restart refinement strategy. Experimental results demonstrate that this methodology outperforms current state-of-the-art approaches across multiple zero-shot conditioning scenarios. ### Critical Evaluation **Novelty:** The paper addresses significant challenges in the realm of text-to-image generation, specifically the limitations associated with training-free feature injection methods. The novel framework that decouples feature injection from denoising processes is a notable innovation, showcasing a unique approach to maintain structural fidelity without additional training. Additionally, the two enhancements (appearance-rich prompting and restart refinement) contribute to the robustness and applicability of the proposed method. **Significance:** The work's significance lies in its potential to improve the quality and fidelity of generated images without the need for extensive training. This is particularly relevant in applications requiring rapid adaptability and efficiency. The reduction of structural misalignment and artifacts could significantly enhance user satisfaction in many creative and industrial applications. **Strengths:** 1. **Innovative Framework:** The decoupling of injection and denoising is a strong conceptual advancement that could lead to more effective image synthesis techniques. 2. **Performance Benchmarks:** The extensive experimental validation and demonstration of state-of-the-art results across diverse scenarios lend credibility to their claims. 3. **Practical Applications:** Training-free methods are highly desirable due to their efficiency, making this approach suitable for real-world applications where resources may be limited. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Limited Discussion on Constraints:** The trade-offs encountered during the decoupling process could be explored more deeply. Understanding the limits of this approach in different contexts would provide a more balanced perspective. 2. **Generalization:** While the results are promising, it would be beneficial to see evaluations on a wider variety of datasets or tasks to better gauge the robustness of the method in less controlled environments. **Overall Influence:** The potential impact of this paper is significant, especially as the demand for sophisticated T2I models grows in various industries like gaming, film, and advertising. However, its full influence will depend on subsequent research and practical implementations that build on these findings. ### Score: 8 This score reflects a strong contribution to the field with clear innovative elements and notable improvements in image generation techniques. The paper excels in proposing a feasible solution to existing problems, while also demonstrating practical applicability. Nevertheless, some areas could benefit from expanded discussion and empirical testing to solidify its standing in the broader context of T2I model development.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Is Reasoning All You Need? Probing Bias in the Age of Reasoning Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02799v1)
- **Authors**: Riccardo Cantini, Nicola Gabriele, Alessio Orsino, Domenico Talia
- **Abstract**: Reasoning Language Models (RLMs) have gained traction for their ability to perform complex, multi-step reasoning tasks through mechanisms such as Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting or fine-tuned reasoning traces. While these capabilities promise improved reliability, their impact on robustness to social biases remains unclear. In this work, we leverage the CLEAR-Bias benchmark, originally designed for Large Language Models (LLMs), to investigate the adversarial robustness of RLMs to bias elicitation. We systematically evaluate state-of-the-art RLMs across diverse sociocultural dimensions, using an LLM-as-a-judge approach for automated safety scoring and leveraging jailbreak techniques to assess the strength of built-in safety mechanisms. Our evaluation addresses three key questions: (i) how the introduction of reasoning capabilities affects model fairness and robustness; (ii) whether models fine-tuned for reasoning exhibit greater safety than those relying on CoT prompting at inference time; and (iii) how the success rate of jailbreak attacks targeting bias elicitation varies with the reasoning mechanisms employed. Our findings reveal a nuanced relationship between reasoning capabilities and bias safety. Surprisingly, models with explicit reasoning, whether via CoT prompting or fine-tuned reasoning traces, are generally more vulnerable to bias elicitation than base models without such mechanisms, suggesting reasoning may unintentionally open new pathways for stereotype reinforcement. Reasoning-enabled models appear somewhat safer than those relying on CoT prompting, which are particularly prone to contextual reframing attacks through storytelling prompts, fictional personas, or reward-shaped instructions. These results challenge the assumption that reasoning inherently improves robustness and underscore the need for more bias-aware approaches to reasoning design.
- **Summary**: **Summary of the Paper:** The study investigates the relationship between reasoning capabilities of Reasoning Language Models (RLMs) and their susceptibility to social biases. Utilizing the CLEAR-Bias benchmark, it systematically evaluates various state-of-the-art RLMs across sociocultural dimensions, examining their robustness against bias elicitation through LLM-as-a-judge safety scoring and jailbreak techniques. The research addresses three primary questions: the influence of reasoning capabilities on fairness and robustness; the relative safety of reasoning-fine-tuned models compared to those utilizing Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting; and the effectiveness of bias elicitation jailbreak attacks based on reasoning methods used. The findings indicate that reasoning mechanisms may inadvertently increase vulnerability to bias elicitation compared to base models. Additionally, while reasoning-enabled models are generally safer than those relying solely on CoT prompting, they still exhibit notable susceptibility, particularly to contextual reframing attacks. The paper emphasizes the necessity for more bias-aware designs in reasoning mechanisms, contradicting the assumption that reasoning capabilities inherently enhance robustness. --- **Critical Evaluation:** **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel examination of RLMs in the context of social biases, a topic that is increasingly critical as language models become more integrated into real-world applications. The alignment of reasoning capabilities with bias elicitation is a fresh perspective that has not been extensively covered in prior research. The findings challenge prevailing assumptions regarding the intrinsic safety of reasoning methods in LLMs, making this a compelling contribution to the field. **Strengths:** 1. **Methodologically Rigorous:** The use of established benchmarks and sophisticated evaluation techniques (e.g., LLM-as-a-judge) lends credibility and robustness to the findings. 2. **Timely Topic:** The investigation addresses an urgent concern in AI ethics and safety, focusing on the real-world implications of enhancing reasoning in language models while potentially amplifying biases. 3. **Clear Implications for Practice:** The results underscore a critical need for redesigns in the development of reasoning capabilities, encouraging a more nuanced approach to mitigate bias hazards in language models. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Limited Generalizability:** The study's findings primarily revolve around the evaluated models; hence, conclusions might not be universally applicable across all RLM architectures or applications. Future work may need to replicate these results across a broader range of models and contexts. 2. **Focused Scope:** While the findings are impactful, they could benefit from a deeper exploration of potential methods for enhancing robustness to biases in reasoning-enhanced models, promoting a more constructive dialogue on corrective strategies. 3. **Potential for Overemphasis:** While the vulnerabilities identified are significant, the paper could risk overstating the dangers of reasoning capabilities without exploring scenarios where reasoning may present benefits, albeit contingent on thoughtful implementation. **Conclusion:** Overall, the paper presents a valuable examination of a critical issue within the AI field, advancing understanding of how reasoning influences bias safety in language models. The insights offered prompt reflection on current methodologies and future model designs, rendering it an important read for researchers and practitioners alike. **Score: 8**  This score reflects the innovative insights presented, timely concerns it raises regarding AI safety, and established methodological rigor, balanced against some limitations in scope and generalizability.
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Time-Masked Transformers with Lightweight Test-Time Adaptation for Neural Speech Decoding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02800v1)
- **Authors**: Ebrahim Feghhi, Shreyas Kaasyap, Nima Hadidi, Jonathan C. Kao
- **Abstract**: Speech neuroprostheses aim to restore communication for people with severe paralysis by decoding speech directly from neural activity. To accelerate algorithmic progress, a recent benchmark released intracranial recordings from a paralyzed participant attempting to speak, along with a baseline decoding algorithm. Prior work on the benchmark showed impressive accuracy gains. However, these gains increased computational costs and were not demonstrated in a real-time decoding setting. Here, we make three contributions that pave the way towards accurate, efficient, and real-time neural speech decoding. First, we incorporate large amounts of time masking during training. On average, over $50\%$ of each trial is masked. Second, we replace the gated recurrent unit (GRU) architecture used in the baseline algorithm with a compact Transformer. The Transformer architecture uses $77\%$ fewer parameters, cuts peak GPU memory usage by $36\%$ relative, and is significantly faster to calibrate relative to the GRU. Third, we design a lightweight variant of an existing test-time adaptation method developed for decoding handwriting from neural activity. Our variant adapts the model using multiple time masked augmentations of a single trial and requires only one gradient step per trial. Together, these contributions reduce word error rate by $19.5\%$ and effectively mitigate performance degradations across held-out days in a real-time decoding setting while substantially lowering computational costs.
- **Summary**: ### Summary The paper presents advancements in neural speech decoding technologies—crucial for speech neuroprostheses aimed at aiding communication for individuals with severe paralysis. It builds upon a benchmark involving intracranial recordings from a participant speaking. The authors contribute three main innovations:  1. **Time Masking**: They implement extensive time masking during training, which obscures over 50% of each trial, potentially reducing overfitting and improving generalizability. 2. **Architecture Change**: The existing gated recurrent unit (GRU) structure is replaced with a more efficient Transformer model, resulting in a 77% reduction in parameters and a 36% decrease in peak GPU memory usage while enhancing calibration speed. 3. **Lightweight Test-Time Adaptation**: They propose a streamlined test-time adaptation method, which allows real-time adaptation using multiple time-masked augmentations and requires only one gradient step per trial. The combination of these contributions leads to a 19.5% reduction in word error rate and significantly improved robustness in real-time settings, highlighting both improved performance and reduced computational demands. ### Critical Evaluation #### Strengths: 1. **Novelty in Approach**: The introduction of extensive time masking and the transformer architecture addresses known issues in speech decoding, such as overfitting and the demand for computational efficiency. 2. **Real-Time Application**: The implementation in real-time situations is a significant advancement since many previous models did not prioritize this factor, which is critical for practical usability. 3. **Efficiency Gains**: The significant parameter and memory usage reductions are noteworthy. This optimizes the decoding process, making it more accessible for real-time applications in clinical settings. #### Weaknesses: 1. **Limited Scope of Evaluation**: While the paper presents improvements, it would benefit from a broader dataset for evaluation to ensure that the findings generalize well across diverse conditions and participants. 2. **Comparative Analysis**: The paper does not delve deeply into a comparative analysis of different architectures beyond the GRU to reveal whether other models could be advantageous. 3. **Complexity of Implementation**: While the proposed methods are lightweight, the underlying complexity of implementation—especially in real-time scenarios—might present challenges in practical applications, which are not fully addressed. ### Conclusion The paper represents a meaningful stride toward advancing the field of neural speech decoding with practical applications in mind, bridging the gap between algorithmic performance and real-world usability. However, greater validation across broader contexts is necessary to fully ascertain the significance and robustness of the proposed methods. **Score: 8**  This score reflects the paper's solid contributions and innovative methodologies while acknowledging the limitations in scope and comparative depth. The work has the potential to influence future developments in neural prosthetics significantly, particularly regarding real-time speech decoding efficiency.
- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 8/10

### Multimodal Mathematical Reasoning with Diverse Solving Perspective
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02804v1)
- **Authors**: Wenhao Shi, Zhiqiang Hu, Yi Bin, Yang Yang, See-Kiong Ng, Heng Tao Shen
- **Abstract**: Recent progress in large-scale reinforcement learning (RL) has notably enhanced the reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs), especially in mathematical domains. However, current multimodal LLMs (MLLMs) for mathematical reasoning often rely on one-to-one image-text pairs and single-solution supervision, overlooking the diversity of valid reasoning perspectives and internal reflections. In this work, we introduce MathV-DP, a novel dataset that captures multiple diverse solution trajectories for each image-question pair, fostering richer reasoning supervision. We further propose Qwen-VL-DP, a model built upon Qwen-VL, fine-tuned with supervised learning and enhanced via group relative policy optimization (GRPO), a rule-based RL approach that integrates correctness discrimination and diversity-aware reward functions. Our method emphasizes learning from varied reasoning perspectives and distinguishing between correct yet distinct solutions. Extensive experiments on the MathVista's minitest and Math-V benchmarks demonstrate that Qwen-VL-DP significantly outperforms prior base MLLMs in both accuracy and generative diversity, highlighting the importance of incorporating diverse perspectives and reflective reasoning in multimodal mathematical reasoning.
- **Summary**: ### Summary of the Paper The paper titled "Multimodal Mathematical Reasoning with Diverse Solving Perspective" addresses shortcomings in current multimodal large language models (MLLMs) pertaining to mathematical reasoning. It recognizes that these models typically utilize single-solution supervision with a focus on one-to-one image-text pairs, thereby neglecting the variety of valid reasoning strategies. To bridge this gap, the authors introduced MathV-DP, a dataset that documents multiple solution trajectories for each image-question pair, thereby enhancing the richness of reasoning supervision. Additionally, the authors propose Qwen-VL-DP, an advanced model built on Qwen-VL, which leverages supervised learning fine-tuning complemented by group relative policy optimization (GRPO)—a novel reinforcement learning strategy that prioritizes both correctness and diversity in solutions. Their extensive experiments across established benchmarks like MathVista's minitest and Math-V reveal significant improvements in both accuracy and reasoning diversity compared to existing models, underscoring the value of embracing diverse perspectives in mathematical reasoning. ### Evaluation **Novelty:** The paper's introduction of MathV-DP is a significant contribution to the field of multimodal learning and mathematical reasoning. The idea of capturing diverse solution trajectories is a marked advancement over the traditional methods, which have generally focused on singular approaches. The proposal of Qwen-VL-DP further builds upon this novelty by integrating the innovative GRPO technique, which not only evaluates correctness but also motivates exploration of different reasoning paths. **Significance:** By pointing out the limitations of existing MLLMs and creating a model that can genuinely understand and incorporate diverse problem-solving methods, this research addresses key challenges in the training of more effective educational AI systems. It opens pathways for developing models that can better assist students with varying cognitive strategies, making the paper quite significant in the context of educational technology and AI. **Strengths:** - The dataset MathV-DP presents a unique resource that can catalyze further research into diverse reasoning strategies. - The proposed GRPO approach is an innovative method that can potentially enhance learning algorithms across various domains beyond mathematics. - The empirical validation on well-regarded benchmarks demonstrates the effectiveness and applicability of their model. **Weaknesses:** - While the dataset and model present advancements, the evaluation could benefit from qualitative analyses of the reasoning applied in diverse solutions to better understand the model's interpretability. - Potential scalability issues regarding the creation of larger datasets with multiple reasoning perspectives may not be fully addressed. - The implementation specifics, including the limitations of GRPO, are not extensively detailed, which may hinder reproducibility and application in other contexts. **Overall Assessment:** This paper contributes noteworthy advancements to multimodal mathematical reasoning through the inclusion of varied problem-solving strategies and showcases how reinforcement learning can enhance model performance. The findings are robust, but certain aspects lack depth in analysis. Nevertheless, the overall innovation and implications make it a strong contender in the field. **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### LangScene-X: Reconstruct Generalizable 3D Language-Embedded Scenes with TriMap Video Diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02813v1)
- **Authors**: Fangfu Liu, Hao Li, Jiawei Chi, Hanyang Wang, Minghui Yang, Fudong Wang, Yueqi Duan
- **Abstract**: Recovering 3D structures with open-vocabulary scene understanding from 2D images is a fundamental but daunting task. Recent developments have achieved this by performing per-scene optimization with embedded language information. However, they heavily rely on the calibrated dense-view reconstruction paradigm, thereby suffering from severe rendering artifacts and implausible semantic synthesis when limited views are available. In this paper, we introduce a novel generative framework, coined LangScene-X, to unify and generate 3D consistent multi-modality information for reconstruction and understanding. Powered by the generative capability of creating more consistent novel observations, we can build generalizable 3D language-embedded scenes from only sparse views. Specifically, we first train a TriMap video diffusion model that can generate appearance (RGBs), geometry (normals), and semantics (segmentation maps) from sparse inputs through progressive knowledge integration. Furthermore, we propose a Language Quantized Compressor (LQC), trained on large-scale image datasets, to efficiently encode language embeddings, enabling cross-scene generalization without per-scene retraining. Finally, we reconstruct the language surface fields by aligning language information onto the surface of 3D scenes, enabling open-ended language queries. Extensive experiments on real-world data demonstrate the superiority of our LangScene-X over state-of-the-art methods in terms of quality and generalizability. Project Page: https://liuff19.github.io/LangScene-X.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper presents LangScene-X, a novel framework designed to reconstruct 3D language-embedded scenes from sparse 2D image inputs. The authors highlight the limitations of existing methods that depend on dense, calibrated views, which often lead to rendering artifacts and poor semantic synthesis. LangScene-X employs a TriMap video diffusion model to generate consistent appearances, geometry, and semantics from limited data. Additionally, a Language Quantized Compressor (LQC) is introduced to efficiently encode language embeddings for generalization across different scenes without requiring individual retraining. The framework aligns language semantics onto the 3D scene structure, allowing open-ended language queries. The experimental results demonstrate the method's superiority over existing state-of-the-art approaches in rendering quality and generalization. **Critical Evaluation:** The novelty of LangScene-X lies in its ability to bridge the gap between language processing and 3D scene reconstruction using sparse inputs, which is a significant advancement in the field of computer vision and language understanding. By employing a combined approach of diffusion models for 3D structure generation and a robust language encoding method, the framework promises improvements over previous dependency on dense-view reconstructions. This is particularly impactful because it allows for scene understanding in situations where only a limited amount of data is available, addressing a critical challenge in the field. However, while the innovations are noteworthy, there are some concerns. The reliance on a pre-trained compressor (LQC) raises questions about how effectively the system adapts to novel situations or complex scenes that may not be well-represented in the initial dataset. Additionally, the paper may benefit from more extensive evaluations across diverse datasets to reinforce claims about generalizability. The potential for rendering artifacts and maintaining the coherence of semantics when transitioning from 2D to 3D is also an area that warrants further discussion. Moreover, the paper's contribution would be enhanced by a thorough comparison with several baseline methods beyond the state-of-the-art, as well as an analysis of computational efficiency and scalability of the proposed approach. In terms of significance, LangScene-X could influence future research directions by emphasizing the integration of language models in 3D reconstructions and facilitating methodology that deals with limited observational data. Nonetheless, without robust validation across multiple types of scenes and varying complexities, the broader applicability of the claims remains somewhat unclear. Overall, while the paper highlights an innovative approach to a critical problem in scene reconstruction, the evaluation of its claims and assurances regarding general applicability and robustness could be improved. **Score: 7**  The score reflects the originality and potential impact of the work while acknowledging the need for further validation and exploration of its practical implications in diverse contexts.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### SynapseRoute: An Auto-Route Switching Framework on Dual-State Large Language Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02822v1)
- **Authors**: Wencheng Zhang, Shiqin Qiao, Lingjie Luo, Yinfeng Li, Chuanyang Zheng, Qian Xu, Meng Li, Yong Gui, Yijun He, Jianing Qiu, Jindong Hong, Jiankai Sun
- **Abstract**: With the widespread adoption of large language models (LLMs) in practical applications, selecting an appropriate model requires balancing not only performance but also operational cost. The emergence of reasoning-capable models has further widened the cost gap between "thinking" (high reasoning) and "non-thinking" (fast, low-cost) modes. In this work, we reveal that approximately 58% of medical questions can be accurately answered by the non-thinking mode alone, without requiring the high-cost reasoning process. This highlights a clear dichotomy in problem complexity and suggests that dynamically routing queries to the appropriate mode based on complexity could optimize accuracy, cost-efficiency, and overall user experience. Based on this, we further propose SynapseRoute, a machine learning-based dynamic routing framework that intelligently assigns input queries to either thinking or non-thinking modes. Experimental results on several medical datasets demonstrate that SynapseRoute not only improves overall accuracy (0.8390 vs. 0.8272) compared to the thinking mode alone but also reduces inference time by 36.8% and token consumption by 39.66%. Importantly, qualitative analysis indicates that over-reasoning on simpler queries can lead to unnecessary delays and even decreased accuracy, a pitfall avoided by our adaptive routing. Finally, this work further introduces the Accuracy-Inference-Token (AIT) index to comprehensively evaluate the trade-offs among accuracy, latency, and token cost.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper presents SynapseRoute, a dynamic routing framework designed to optimize the performance of dual-state large language models (LLMs) in the context of medical queries. It highlights that around 58% of medical questions can be effectively answered using a low-cost, non-thinking mode, which contrasts with the high-cost, reasoning-intensive thinking mode. SynapseRoute intelligently routes queries depending on their complexity, resulting in improved accuracy (0.8390 vs. 0.8272), a 36.8% decrease in inference time, and a 39.66% reduction in token usage when compared to the thinking mode alone. The authors introduce a new performance metric, the Accuracy-Inference-Token (AIT) index, to evaluate the balance between accuracy, latency, and token usage. **Critical Evaluation:** The novelty of SynapseRoute lies in its approach to dynamically switch between different operational modes based on the complexity of input queries. This method reflects a well-considered innovation given the increasing demand for cost-effective AI solutions, particularly in healthcare. By demonstrating that a substantial portion of medical inquiries do not require deep reasoning, the paper effectively emphasizes the potential for efficiency gains through strategic mode selection. This contrasts with conventional approaches, which typically engage high-cost reasoning for all types of queries without consideration of complexity. However, while the concept is compelling and the empirical results are promising, there are several limitations. First, the research primarily focuses on medical datasets, which may limit the generalizability of the findings to other domains. The reliance on specific datasets raises questions about the versatility of SynapseRoute across diverse applications outside of healthcare. Moreover, the qualitative analyses regarding over-reasoning could benefit from more extensive datasets and scenarios, especially given the inherent variability in natural language queries. Additionally, while the AIT index is a noteworthy contribution for understanding trade-offs, it must be validated in a broader context with more comprehensive benchmarks to ensure its utility across various LLM applications. In summary, SynapseRoute represents a meaningful step towards optimizing LLMs for cost and performance, especially within the medical domain. However, the paper's impact could be more robust with broader validation, and a detailed exploration of potential applications in various fields. **Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### USAD: An Unsupervised Data Augmentation Spatio-Temporal Attention Diffusion Network
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02827v1)
- **Authors**: Ying Yu, Hang Xiao, Siyao Li, Jiarui Li, Haotian Tang, Hanyu Liu, Chao Li
- **Abstract**: The primary objective of human activity recognition (HAR) is to infer ongoing human actions from sensor data, a task that finds broad applications in health monitoring, safety protection, and sports analysis. Despite proliferating research, HAR still faces key challenges, including the scarcity of labeled samples for rare activities, insufficient extraction of high-level features, and suboptimal model performance on lightweight devices. To address these issues, this paper proposes a comprehensive optimization approach centered on multi-attention interaction mechanisms. First, an unsupervised, statistics-guided diffusion model is employed to perform data augmentation, thereby alleviating the problems of labeled data scarcity and severe class imbalance. Second, a multi-branch spatio-temporal interaction network is designed, which captures multi-scale features of sequential data through parallel residual branches with 3*3, 5*5, and 7*7 convolutional kernels. Simultaneously, temporal attention mechanisms are incorporated to identify critical time points, while spatial attention enhances inter-sensor interactions. A cross-branch feature fusion unit is further introduced to improve the overall feature representation capability. Finally, an adaptive multi-loss function fusion strategy is integrated, allowing for dynamic adjustment of loss weights and overall model optimization. Experimental results on three public datasets, WISDM, PAMAP2, and OPPORTUNITY, demonstrate that the proposed unsupervised data augmentation spatio-temporal attention diffusion network (USAD) achieves accuracies of 98.84%, 93.81%, and 80.92% respectively, significantly outperforming existing approaches. Furthermore, practical deployment on embedded devices verifies the efficiency and feasibility of the proposed method.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper titled "USAD: An Unsupervised Data Augmentation Spatio-Temporal Attention Diffusion Network" addresses challenges in human activity recognition (HAR), particularly the lack of labeled samples for rare activities and insufficient high-level feature extraction. It proposes a novel model, USAD, which combines an unsupervised statistics-guided diffusion model for data augmentation, a multi-branch spatio-temporal interaction network to capture multi-scale features, temporal and spatial attention mechanisms for enhancing feature representation, and an adaptive multi-loss function fusion strategy for model optimization.
